# TAOM.CoopCompat (for TAOM interop)

> **Purpose:** the durable record of what the third-party `TAOM.CoopCompat` module does, so no
> future session has to decompile it. Read this before changing anything it binds to, and before
> cutting a TAOM release: the module pins TAOM's exact build identity, so a release that ignores
> it takes co-op down.
>
> **Subject:** `TAOM.CoopCompat` **v0.3.15**, authored by **Sternab**, shipped as a CLIENT
> PLAYTEST and a HOST OPERATOR bundle. Pinned tuple: Bannerlord **v1.4.8**, TAOM **v2.0.26**,
> BannerlordCoop **v0.1.4**, Compat **v0.3.15**, `allPeersRequireExactCompat: true`.
>
> **Not the same thing as `Main/Features/CoopInterop/`.** TAOM's in-repo layer decides which TAOM
> behaviour stands down under co-op. This module makes the stood-down behaviour work across
> peers. See [Suppression versus replication](#suppression-versus-replication-two-projects-opposite-jobs).
>
> **Licensing:** BannerlordCoop is source-available, not open source
> ([Licensing and provenance](#licensing-and-provenance)). Nothing in TAOM derives from either
> project.
>
> **Mechanically extracted evidence** (component table, every pin, hashes, counts):
> [`docs/raw/taom-coopcompat/extracted-evidence.json`](../raw/taom-coopcompat/extracted-evidence.json).


## Provenance and confidence

**No source was supplied.** Everything rests on two shipped assemblies, the provenance JSONs beside them, and the operator scripts; the private tree appears only as 350 SHA-256 hashes.

| Artifact | What it gave us |
|---|---|
| `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` (788,992 bytes) | [Verified] Decompiled to **313 `.cs` files**; 312 declare a namespace across **6**: `TAOM.CoopCompat.Core` (144), `.Components` (100), `.Relay` (50), `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat` (11), `Coop.Core.Server.Services.TaomCoopCompat` (6), `TAOM.CoopCompat` (1, `CoopCompatSubModule.cs`). The 313th is `Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs`, which declares none. |
| `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` (14,336 bytes) | [Verified] 5 `.cs` files: `BootstrapSubModule.cs`, `CoopDependencyBridge.cs`, `CoopDependencyBridgePolicy.cs`, `CoopDependencyPin.cs`, `Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs`. |
| `Evidence/TAOM.CoopCompat-SOURCE-MANIFEST.json` | [Verified] **350** path to SHA-256 entries, matching `ARTIFACT.json` `sourceFileCount: 350`. **228 under `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/`** (Components 95, Core 72, Relay 45, Bootstrap 4, 12 loose), **111 under `tests/`** (86 `TAOM.CoopCompat.Tests`, 13 fixtures, 10 across 4 smoke projects, 2 loose `.ps1`), 11 `tools/*.ps1`. By extension: 307 `.cs`, 14 `.ps1`, 10 `.xml`, 7 `.csproj`, 5 `.md`, 4 `.mbproj`, 2 `.txt`, 1 `.json`. |
| `PACKAGE.json` | [Verified] Supported tuple, 5 protocol version numbers, pinned Coop and TAOM hashes, 43 `liveAcceptance` keys, **33** `knownOpen` entries. Both packages ship the same file: 17,815 bytes, SHA-256 `b435063f…c2de`. |
| `Evidence/TAOM.CoopCompat-ARTIFACT.json` | [Verified] `gitHead` `1151ac496a5afb91bfeb87c71fcb9934fd431894`, `configuration` `Release`, `sourceFileCount` 350, 113-entry `sourceStatus`. Byte-identical to the host copy at `Artifacts/TAOM.CoopCompat/0.3.15+340a0657509dd1ec/ARTIFACT.json`. Its `note`: `NOT release-qualified: headless bootstrap and integration gates pending`. |
| Host PowerShell tooling | [Verified] 12 files, **4,670 lines** (`Stage-TaomCompatServer.ps1` 1,329, `Prepare-Host.ps1` 869, `Collect-HostLogs.ps1` 702, `SERVER-MANAGER.ps1` 532, and 8 smaller). Read as text with `sed`/`grep`, nothing executed. |
| Client PowerShell tooling | [Verified] 3 files, **2,840 lines** (`Install-Client.ps1` 1,262, `Collect-ClientLogs.ps1` 903, `Rollback-Client.ps1` 675). Read, never run. |
| Package text | [Verified] Client: `README-FIRST.txt`, `WHAT-CHANGED.txt`, `KNOWN-ISSUES.txt`, `LIVE-TEST-SUMMARY.txt`. Host: those four plus `HOST-REDISTRIBUTION-NOTICE.txt`, `SERVER-SETTINGS.txt`. Of the four shared names only `README-FIRST.txt` differs (2,093 bytes client, 1,953 host). |

[Verified] Decompilation used `ilspycmd` **9.1.0.7988** / `ICSharpCode.Decompiler` **9.1.0.7988** (read from the tool on PATH this pass; project-mode output carries no embedded stamp, so the identity is the installed tool). The sibling node records `ilspycmd` **10.0.1**: the two Coop nodes in this repo came from different decompiler majors.

**Hash chain, checked rather than assumed.** [Verified] `sha256sum` on the shipped client `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` returns `86acfbc4…53a4`, matching `PACKAGE.json`, `ARTIFACT.json` and both packages' `CHECKSUMS.sha256`; Bootstrap is `6eb31bcb…42e3`. The pair sits in **four slot directories** (`Win64_Shipping_Client` and `Win64_Shipping_Server` in each package), so 8 DLL files resolve to 2 distinct binaries and there is no separate server build. The manifest hashes to `abbdf9ad…5bfd`, matching `sourceManifestSha256`.

**What was not available:**
- **The source tree.** [Verified] The 228 `src/` paths exist only as hashes; every claim about Compat behaviour comes from decompiled IL, not the author's code.
- **`Coop.Core.dll`.** [Verified] Neither package ships it. Present DLLs: the two Compat assemblies (4 slots, 8 files), `DedicatedServer.Core.dll`, `DedicatedServer.Windows.dll`, `Tools/Mono.Cecil.dll`; the host also carries `BannerlordCoopServer.exe` (33,400,014 bytes, not decompiled). `PACKAGE.json` pins `Coop.Core.dll` at `45924C8D…FB2` without carrying it. Compat declares `IHandler` types in `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat` and `Coop.Core.Server.Services.TaomCoopCompat`, and the ILSpy-emitted `.csproj` references `Common`, `GameInterface`, `Missions`, `LiteNetLib`, `protobuf-net.Core`, `Serilog`, `0Harmony`, `TAOM` and seven TaleWorlds assemblies but **not** `Coop.Core`. [Inference] `IHandler` resolves out of `Common` (handler files carry `using Common;`, `using Common.Messaging;`, `using Common.Network;`); which of the three namespaces owns it needs `Common.dll`. [Inference] Handler bodies are fully readable, Coop-side discovery and registration of them are not confirmable from this payload.
- **Runtime evidence.** [Verified] `diagnostics.status` is `static-qualified-runtime-unproven`, `runtimeProven` and `runtimeQualified` are `false`, and of the 43 `liveAcceptance` keys 42 are booleans, every one `false`; the 43rd is `status`, reading `v0.3.15-coop-v0.1.4-migration-runtime-unproven`. `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` claims 817 Compat tests, 303 client and 246 dedicated CLR settings checks passed; no run is in the package, so that is the author's report, not evidence held here.
- **An independent live-test record.** [Verified] `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` and `LIVE-TEST-SUMMARY.txt` are byte-identical (`cmp` clean, 2,134 bytes each, same digest `106A3D75…` in `CHECKSUMS.sha256`). Treat them as one document.
- **A clean build provenance.** [Verified] `sourceDirty` is `true` for `TAOM.CoopCompat` (`sourceTreeSha256` `17F6243B…D459F99`, `gitHead` `1151ac49…`) and `false` for the wrapper. `sourceStatus`: **113 entries, 37 modified, 1 deleted, 75 untracked.** The DLL was built from that dirty tree, not from `1151ac49` as committed.

**Dates and version basis.** [Verified] Both packages record `createdUtc` **2026-09-05T12:46:26.1003996Z**; analysis was **2026-09-07**. The tuple: Bannerlord **v1.4.8**, TAOM **v2.0.26**, Coop **v0.1.4** (`coopIdentity` `workshop-3770450698-v0.1.4-20260830`), Compat **v0.3.15**, revision `r4-visible-server-launch`, package suffix `340a0657-8295bcb5-r2`.

[Verified] This is **not** the Coop build in the sibling node: [`bannerlordcoop-internals.md`](bannerlordcoop-internals.md) analysed Coop **v0.0.3** on game **v1.4.7**, module id `Coop`, upstream commit `52e5ed70`. Same Workshop id `3770450698`, two releases apart. Any Coop-side mechanism cited from that node is [Inference] carrying version risk until re-read against v0.1.4.

**Packaging defect, know it before quoting either file.** [Verified] The HOST-OPERATOR package's `PACKAGE.json` carries `packageId: TAOM-Coop-CompatOnly-Client-Playtest`, the client id. Not a copied field: the two `PACKAGE.json` files are byte-identical (17,815 bytes, `b435063f…c2de`). Nothing inside distinguishes the roles; the directory name and payload do.

**The author.** [Inference] The module is by **Sternab**, an existing TAOM contributor; that attribution is out-of-band and should be sourced in this document's header before it is relied on. `Sternab` appears nowhere in either package (case-insensitive `grep -ril` returns zero hits; a byte scan of `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` finds zero ASCII and zero UTF-16LE hits), and `TAOM.CoopCompat` appears nowhere in this repo's `CHANGELOG.md`. The work is external to the repo and unversioned here.

[Verified] Sternab's 4 `CHANGELOG.md` mentions: PR **#429** (issue #425), the exit-stall sampler fix under `fix(diagnostics): the exit-stall sampler was watching player time (#425, PR #429)`, rebuilt locally because CI skips `Build & Test` when `BANNERLORD_GAME_DIR` is unset, 6276 passed / 0 failed / 2 skipped; PR **#426** (issue #424), the enlistment fix where a private was handed to the engine as general of his battle side, commit `00d39113` merged as `6dc9dadc`; issue **#403**, the dwarf-female underwear mesh defect (skin asks `sk_dwarf_underwear_female`, armory ships `sk_dwarf_underwear_female_a`) and its evidence; the `lint_docs` merge (#397, #399), the review table over @davrodwconnections' external contribution.

[Verified] The assembly leaks its build path two ways. `GameThread.RunSafe` (19 call sites) and `GameThread.EnqueueSafe` (3) bake in a caller file path, so strings like `C:\Users\<author>\Documents\Bannerlord Coop\src\TAOM.CoopCompat\Relay\AuthoritativePositionClientHandler.cs` sit in the shipped DLL: **22 occurrences, 12 distinct paths, across 9 client-side and 3 server-side handler files**, and all 12 tails resolve against manifest `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Relay/…` entries (12 of 12), corroborating a real private tree rather than a synthesized listing. Both DLLs also carry a CodeView `RSDS` PDB record: `…\src\TAOM.CoopCompat\obj\Release\TAOM.CoopCompat.pdb` and `…\src\TAOM.CoopCompat\Bootstrap\obj\Release\TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.pdb`. [Inference] `<author>` is the only author trace in either binary, appears nowhere in this repo's `docs/` or `CHANGELOG.md`, and does not by itself connect to the Sternab identity.

**Confidence policy.** **[Verified]** = read directly this pass, in decompiled source, a provenance JSON, a script read as text, or the output of a command run this pass. **[Inference]** = reasoned from what was read, traceable to named evidence but not itself read. Anything traceable to neither was cut rather than softened.

[Verified] One decompiler caveat on the [Verified] tier: **93 of the 313** files carry ILSpy `//IL_xxxx:` annotations, **757 lines** in all, of which **570** are `Unknown result type (might be due to invalid IL or missing references)`, 146 `Expected O, but got Unknown`, 36 `Invalid comparison between Unknown and I4`, 5 `Expected I4, but got Unknown`. They concentrate where Coop and TaleWorlds types could not be resolved: control flow there is readable, operand types are not, so claims drawn from those methods are marked [Inference]. Three files decompile to raw `goto IL_xxxx` labels rather than structured control flow.

## What TAOM.CoopCompat is

`TAOM.CoopCompat` is a third-party Bannerlord module, built and distributed outside this repo, whose only job is to keep a TAOM campaign alive under BannerlordCoop's authoritative-server model. Not a fork, ships no TAOM content: one module folder, two assemblies, 55 checksummed files across two bundles, three JSON records, a 350-entry source manifest. Build commits `1151ac496a5afb91bfeb87c71fcb9934fd431894` (Compat) and `c49ba6f6ba5134872a6dc1cd8c4af1806dcc7f40` (wrapper) both return `could not get object info` from `git cat-file -t` inside TAOM, and the manifest's paths are `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/...` and `tests/...`, not `Main/Features/...` [Verified].

**The two bundles** sit in `C:\Users\mikew\Downloads`, both suffixed `340a0657-8295bcb5-r2`, each a `.zip` beside its extracted folder [Verified]:

| Bundle | ZIP SHA-256, bytes | Top level | Entry + ops + unique text | Tools | CHECKSUMS |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CLIENT-PLAYTEST | `010279391c2b1230553c3bd8618c660bee2c95155f7f70674fe7a612b4a0e76f`, 638,009 | 10 files; `Evidence/`, `Payload/`, `Tools/` | `INSTALL-OR-UPDATE.cmd`, `ROLLBACK.cmd`, `COLLECT-LOGS.cmd`; `PACKAGE.template.json` (9,628 B) | 3 PS1: `Install-Client.ps1`, `Rollback-Client.ps1`, `Collect-ClientLogs.ps1` | 19 lines, 19 OK |
| HOST-OPERATOR | `10306d0cae686127f6a06c9bf0f512601ccde064490af129287188132fbf27b0`, 29,829,374 | 12 files; `Artifacts/`, `Tools/` | `PREPARE-HOST.cmd`, `SERVER-MANAGER.cmd` + `SERVER-MANAGER.ps1` (26,252 B), `COLLECT-HOST-LOGS.cmd`; `HOST-REDISTRIBUTION-NOTICE.txt`, `SERVER-SETTINGS.txt` | 12 files: `Prepare-Host.ps1`, `Stage-TaomCompatServer.ps1`, `Host-QualificationPolicy.ps1`, `Repair-TaomServerGameVersion.ps1`, `Export-Taom2026BattleTerrain.ps1`, `New-StrippedMapScene.ps1`, vendored `Mono.Cecil.dll` (373,584 B) | 36 lines, 36 OK |

`sha256sum -c` passes 19 of 19 and 36 of 36 with zero failures, but the manifests are CRLF: pipe the input through `tr -d '\r'` or every line fails on "No such file or directory" [Verified]. Both bundles carry the same five doc/provenance files and four are byte-identical across them (`PACKAGE.json`, `KNOWN-ISSUES.txt`, `WHAT-CHANGED.txt`, `LIVE-TEST-SUMMARY.txt` all pass `diff -q`); only `README-FIRST.txt` differs, each naming its own entry script. `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` and `LIVE-TEST-SUMMARY.txt` are byte-identical to each other (2,134 B), so the "live test summary" is the changelog text under a second filename, not independent runtime evidence [Verified].

**Suffix provenance:** `PACKAGE.json` carries `"packageSuffix": "340a0657-8295bcb5-r2"` as its own field, and its two hex groups are exact leading-8 substrings of the Compat artifact directory `0.3.15+340a0657509dd1ec` and the wrapper artifact id `taom-coop-wrapper-8295bcb5816ef9b6`. The trailing `-r2` is unexplained by anything in either bundle and disagrees with the same file's `packageRevision` value `r4-visible-server-launch` [Verified; the disagreement is unresolved].

**Host `Artifacts/`** (what the client bundle lacks): `BannerlordCoopServer.exe` 33,400,014 B, a `DedicatedServer.Windows` module (`DedicatedServer.Core.dll`, `DedicatedServer.Windows.dll`), `Mapping.txt`, `WRAPPER-ARTIFACT.json`, plus a second copy of the Compat artifact under `Artifacts/TAOM.CoopCompat/0.3.15+340a0657509dd1ec/` [Verified]. `HOST-REDISTRIBUTION-NOTICE.txt` says of the **Host ZIP specifically** that "It does not redistribute Bannerlord, TAOM or Workshop Coop content" and that the preparer reads those installed sources into a separate server tree "after exact version and hash checks pass"; it never extends that non-redistribution sentence to the Client ZIP, claiming only that the client "contains only the TAOM.CoopCompat module and its installer, diagnostics and provenance records" (true by inspection of its 5-file `Payload/`) [Verified]. The notice calls the wrapper "qualified"; its own record does not: `WRAPPER-ARTIFACT.json` has `status` `"static-qualified"`, `runtimeQualified` `false`, `obfuscated: true` (corroborated by the 1,378-line `Mapping.txt` rename map beside it), and the note "Static build/IL qualification only; SERVING, save/reload, and client joins are not proven." Its `moduleToken` fixes the expected load set: `Native`, `SandBoxCore`, `Sandbox`, `StoryMode`, `CustomBattle`, `TAOM.Dependencies`, `TAOM`, `TAOM_Map`, `LOTRLOME_Armory`, `CoopNightly`, `TAOM.CoopCompat`, `DedicatedServer.Windows` [Verified].

**The pinned tuple.** `PACKAGE.json` (`schemaVersion` 2, `packageId` `TAOM-Coop-CompatOnly-Client-Playtest`, `packageVersion` `0.3.15`, `packageRevision` `r4-visible-server-launch`, `createdUtc` `2026-09-05T12:46:26.1003996Z`) pins `"tuple": { "bannerlord": "v1.4.8", "taom": "v2.0.26", "coop": "v0.1.4", "compat": "v0.3.15", "variant": "compat-only" }` with `allPeersRequireExactCompat: true`, `launcherPolicy` `"exact-supported-tuple"`, and `launcherOrder` exactly `["CoopNightly", "TAOM.CoopCompat"]` [Verified]. The pinning goes below the version strings: `supportedBaseline` records `taomDllSha256` `7441F277EACC16EE...`, a `taomDependenciesDllSha256` list holding exactly one hash, a `battleSceneRegistry` entry for `TAOM/ModuleData/sp_battle_scenes.xml` with its exact length (12,070 B) and hash, a `battleTerrainPackage` pinning `TAOM_Map/AssetPackages/pack4.tpac` at 905,846,252 B with a 276,194-B staged derivative, `coopIdentity` `"workshop-3770450698-v0.1.4-20260830"`, and five per-DLL `coopPins`: `Common.dll`, `Coop.Core.dll`, `Coop.Steam.dll`, `GameInterface.dll`, `Missions.dll` [Verified].

**Protocol counters** under `protocols`: `authoritativePartyPosition` 2, `careerAuthority` 1, `fullWarDiplomacy` 1, `specialResourceSpend` 2, `taomServerSettings` 2 [Verified]. The last is contradicted inside the same file: `knownOpen[0]` reads "0.3.15 changes TAOM settings protocol to 3; all peers must update", and `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` agrees with `knownOpen`, not with the `protocols` field [Verified as a contradiction; which counter is authoritative is unresolved and belongs to a read of the Compat DLL's settings handler].

**TAOM seams it names, both real identifiers here:** `taomCoopPolicyFlags` `["coop-force-active.flag", "patchshield-disabled.flag"]` are `ForceActiveFlagName` at `Dependencies/Foundation/CoopPresence.cs:47` and `DisableFlagName` at `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:37`; `taomCoopModuleIds` is `["Coop", "CoopNightly"]` [Verified].

**Self-declared confidence** [Verified, all quoted from `PACKAGE.json`]: `status` `"public-playtest-candidate"`; `wrapper.runtimeQualified` `false`; `wrapper.sourceDirty` `false`; `wrapper.coreTests` `"71/71"` and `wrapper.launcherTests` `"15/15"`; `modules["TAOM.CoopCompat"].sourceDirty` **`true`** with `.runtimeQualified` `false`; `diagnostics.status` `"static-qualified-runtime-unproven"`; `diagnostics.runtimeProven` `false`; `liveAcceptance.status` `"v0.3.15-coop-v0.1.4-migration-runtime-unproven"`. So the shipped Compat DLL was built from a working tree with uncommitted changes, and that is not an inference: `Evidence/TAOM.CoopCompat-ARTIFACT.json` carries a `sourceStatus` array of **113 git-status porcelain lines** beside `sourceDirty: true` (` M src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Bootstrap/CoopDependencyBridge.cs`, ` D src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Components/EncounterArmyMapEventGuardComponent.cs`, `?? tools/Set-TaomCoopInteropPolicy.ps1`, and 110 more) while the wrapper's record has `sourceStatus` length 0, and it closes with `note` "NOT release-qualified: headless bootstrap and integration gates pending" [Verified].

`liveAcceptance` holds **43 keys: 42 booleans, every one `false`**, plus the `status` string. 40 of the booleans end in `RuntimeProven` (`patchShieldRuntimeProven`, `careerAuthorityRuntimeProven`, `serializationGraphLifetimeRuntimeProven`, and 37 more); the two that do not are `battleHangRootCauseResolved` and `treatmentWrapperRuntimeProvenForV025`. `knownOpen` has 33 entries [Verified by enumeration]. `KNOWN-ISSUES.txt` lists 11 bulleted areas that "have static regression coverage but still need multiplayer runtime evidence", one being "large-battle hard hangs (new heartbeat diagnostics, not a claimed cure)"; the flatter "This release does not claim to fix the previously reported large-battle hard hang" is in `WHAT-CHANGED.txt`, not `KNOWN-ISSUES.txt` [Verified]. The claimed counts are static, from one `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` paragraph: "817 Compat tests, 303 client CLR settings checks, 246 dedicated CLR settings checks, and Server Manager/collector regression tests" (the fourth item carries no number), caveated "They do not prove every campaign trigger or multiplayer condition in-game" [Verified].

**The payload** is exactly one module folder and five files under `Payload/Modules/TAOM.CoopCompat/`: `SubModule.xml`, plus `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` and `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` in each of `bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/` and `bin/Win64_Shipping_Server/`. The two role folders hold the **same bytes**, not merely the same version: `sha256sum` over all four DLLs gives two distinct digests [Verified].

| File | SHA-256 | Bytes |
|---|---|---|
| `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` (both roles) | `6eb31bcb677b924054d31ac8e1c10f6083309f81e37031faba26b535e79442e3` | 14,336 |
| `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` (both roles) | `86acfbc45af5928d66fcd91aa0fbd274bf998e1ce10438e5fecc2cd0d5af53a4` | 788,992 |
| `SubModule.xml` | `dd061c16126430b17e008023cece94e679e5bd525bf4b52bbc63cb7f7cb37e00` | 2,122 |

Those digests match `PACKAGE.json`'s `modules.TAOM.CoopCompat.payloadFiles`, match `Evidence/TAOM.CoopCompat-ARTIFACT.json`, and match the host bundle's `Artifacts/` copy of the same five files byte for byte [Verified]. One build serves both roles.

**`SubModule.xml`:** `Id` `TAOM.CoopCompat`, `Name` `TAOM Coop Compatibility`, `Version` `v0.3.15`, `SingleplayerModule="true"`, `MultiplayerModule="false"`, `DependedModules` = `Native`, `TAOM.Dependencies`, `TAOM`, `LOTRLOME_Armory`, `CoopNightly` [Verified]. A comment explains the naming: "No DedicatedServer. prefix: this module patches simulation, so Coop's exact-version validation MUST include it on every machine" [Verified, quoted]. A separate `DependedModuleMetadatas` block pins order and versions, all `LoadBeforeThis`: `TAOM` `v2.0.23`, `TAOM.Dependencies` `v2.0.23`, `CoopNightly` `v0.1.4`, `LOTRLOME_Armory` (no version), plus two entries the dependency list does not carry, optional `Alliance.Wargs` and optional `TAOM_Map` (no versions) [Verified]. **Those `v2.0.23` pins contradict every other statement of the required TAOM version in the bundle**: `tuple.taom` is `v2.0.26`, `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` says "Requires TAOM 2.0.26", and both `README-FIRST.txt` files list "TAOM 2.0.26" [Verified as a contradiction; stale floor vs live gate is not answerable from the XML alone]. Two `SubModule` entries load in a fixed order, `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.BootstrapSubModule` first because "Its constructor establishes exact Coop dependency bindings before Bannerlord scans the following assembly's TypeDefs", then `TAOM.CoopCompat.CoopCompatSubModule`; a third comment records that the second deliberately carries no `Tags`, on an IL check dated 2026-08-10 (the client loader accepts only `IsNoRenderModeElement="false"`, the no-render server loader only `"true"`, and "an EMPTY tag list short-circuits to loadable everywhere") [Verified as the file's claim; the IL check is the author's and is not reproduced here].

**`Evidence/TAOM.CoopCompat-SOURCE-MANIFEST.json`** hashes the build's source tree without reproducing it: **350 files, 307 of them `.cs`**, plus 14 `.ps1`, 10 `.xml`, 7 `.csproj`, 5 `.md`, 4 `.mbproj`, 2 `.txt`, 1 `.json`; split `src` 228, `tests` 111, `tools` 11; largest directories `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Components` 95, `tests/TAOM.CoopCompat.Tests` 86, `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Core` 72, `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Relay` 45, `tests/fixtures/taom-2.0.20` 13, `tools` 11, `src/TAOM.CoopCompat` root files 10, `src/TAOM.CoopCompat/Bootstrap` 4 [Verified by counting the manifest's `files` map, its only top-level key]. The only TAOM fixtures are under `tests/fixtures/taom-2.0.20`, with zero paths mentioning `2.0.26`: the regression corpus is pinned six patch versions behind the tuple the package requires [Verified].

**The TAOM repo has never heard of it.** `git grep -in "coopcompat"` returns **0 tracked hits** and `git log --all -i --grep="coopcompat"` returns **0 commits**; a plain `grep -rn -i "coopcompat" . --exclude-dir=.git` returns **68 lines**, every one in `docs/raw/taom-coopcompat/extracted-evidence.json`, which this research pass wrote today and which is untracked but not gitignored (`git check-ignore -v` exits 1; `git status --porcelain` shows `?? docs/raw/taom-coopcompat/`). The honest statement is "nothing in tracked content or git history", not "nothing on disk" [Verified]. Detection is one-way: `Dependencies/_Module/coop-modules.txt` lists exactly `BannerlordTogether`, `BattleLinkMPClient`, `Coop` under `[modules]`, with `[harmony-owner-prefixes]` empty by design, and since its header says entries "are ADDED to TAOM's compiled defaults" the txt alone would not settle `CoopNightly`; `git grep -in "CoopNightly"` returning **0 tracked hits** does settle it [Verified]. Compat pins TAOM by DLL hash, asset hash, flag file and module id; TAOM names neither `CoopNightly` nor `TAOM.CoopCompat` anywhere.

**Authorship.** The bundles contain no author string: `grep -rai "sternab"` over both extracted bundles returns nothing [Verified]. The attribution rests on this repo, where a case-insensitive `git grep` for `sternab` returns 8 tracked lines across 5 files naming `@Sternab` as an external contributor and reviewer: PR #429 (`CHANGELOG.md:9389`), a merge crediting `00d39113` as `6dc9dadc` (`CHANGELOG.md:9705`), a review table (`CHANGELOG.md:11213`), and the #403 female-dwarf mesh defect (`CHANGELOG.md:11252`, `docs/reference/lotrlome-armory-snapshot/README.md:194`, `docs/reviews/rca-patch69-tournament-guard-2026-08-07.md:4`) [Verified]. That corroborates the attribution; it does not prove it, because the bundle is unsigned and carries no author field [Inference].

## The version treadmill: why a TAOM release bricks co-op

TAOM.CoopCompat does not read TAOM's module version. It pins the **ModuleVersionId**, the GUID the C# compiler stamps into every assembly it emits, so the compat is bound to one exact `TAOM.dll` build rather than to a version range.

**Scope.** [Verified] `CoopDependencyBridge.InstallAndPreload` throws `"TAOM.CoopCompat bootstrap requires the active Coop module."` unless `ModuleHelper.GetModuleInfo("CoopNightly")` returns an active module, so a player without the compat is unaffected. [Verified] Neither `CoopNightly` nor `TAOM.CoopCompat` is in this machine's 18-entry `Modules/` listing, so every compat fact below comes from the supplied decompile while TAOM and engine facts are measured off local binaries. Single-player TAOM is untouched by every failure path here.

### The pins

[Verified] `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent.cs` declares four constants (lines 13, 15, 17, 19) and `Critical => true` (25): `ExpectedAssemblyFullName = "TAOM, Version=2.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=null"`, `ExpectedModuleVersionId = "bd1af67e-d002-47d1-bf58-83e8967fd143"`, `ExpectedRefreshIlLength = 295`, `ExpectedExecuteIlLength = 54`. `RequireFrozenTaomAssembly` ordinal-compares `typeof(FieldCampOverlayVM).Assembly`'s full name and `ManifestModule.ModuleVersionId` against those two strings and throws `InvalidOperationException("TAOM FieldCamp overlay is outside the frozen 2.0.26 contract (assembly=…, mvid=…)")`. `RequireTarget` independently rejects null, static, non-void or wrong-`GetILAsByteArray().Length` seams with `MissingMethodException("exact TAOM 2.0.26 FieldCampOverlayVM.<name> seam was not found")`. [Verified] `Install` order: mode check (34), `RequireFrozenTaomAssembly()` (38), two `RequireTarget` (39, 40), two `harmony.Patch`, two `RequireOwnedPatch`. The identity gate is unconditionally first, so the IL gate is unreachable once the MVID misses.

[Verified] `SiegeDefenseTelemetryComponent.cs:37` carries the same GUID as `private const string TaomMvid`, enforced by `ResolveTaomSnapshotStart` before it resolves `TAOM.Adapters.MapEventBattleLogAdapter.SnapshotStart(MapEvent)`; the call site at `:122` passes inlined literals, not the named constants. It is `Critical => false` (49) with `DefaultMode => ComponentMode.Observe`, so in `ComponentRegistry.InstallAll` the throw is caught, the component is unpatched and deactivated, `SafeCritical` returns false, `MarkFaulted` is never reached and the loop continues. Both resolves run before either `harmony.Patch`, so nothing is left half patched. Telemetry dies, the session does not. [Verified] `grep -rln bd1af67e` over both trees returns exactly those two files and zero in the bootstrap tree. [Verified] `main/Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs:9`: `[assembly: AssemblyInformationalVersion("0.3.15-taom-2.0.26-authoritative-party-settings")]`.

**Config cannot dodge a pin.** [Verified] `CompatConfig` parses `coop-taom.json` from the module root and accepts exactly `schemaVersion`, `protocolVersion` and `components`; any other key is an error and an invalid config forces every component off. Each component takes `enabled`, `observe` or `off`, and a legacy `<name>-disabled.flag` in the module root or bin dir also forces off. [Verified] But `InstallAll` (74 to 79) refuses a critical component in any non-`Enabled` mode before it calls `Install`, logging `critical component requested as {mode}; session faulted` and calling `CompatState.MarkFaulted`; the component agrees from its own side, throwing `"field-camp co-op suppression must be enabled"`. Setting `field-camp-coop-suppression` to off produces the same fault as failing its MVID pin.

[Verified] Rows 1 to 6 are the only `const int` IL-length pins in either tree; rows 7 to 11 are the four components that go past length to a SHA-256 of the IL bytes. Nine files in total call `GetILAsByteArray`. **There is no shared hash helper:** `RequireIlHash` is private static at `RaidLootIsolationComponent.cs:229` with four call sites in that same file (154, 173, 192, 211); the other three build their own `SHA256.Create()` block inline (`CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent.cs:139` and `:160`, `CatchUpDrainGuardComponent.cs:113`, `HeroHomeSettlementDiagnosticCoalescerComponent.cs:132`).

| Component | Pinned seam | Pin | Qualified assembly | Critical |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `field-camp-coop-suppression` | `FieldCampOverlayVM.Refresh` | IL 295 | TAOM | true (25) |
| `field-camp-coop-suppression` | `FieldCampOverlayVM.ExecuteOpenCampMenu` | IL 54 | TAOM | true |
| `recruitment-volunteer-null-guard` | `RecruitmentCampaignBehavior.UpdateVolunteersOfNotablesInSettlement` | IL 574 | `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` | true (37) |
| `transferred-save-troop-training-guard` | `DefaultPartyTrainingModel.GetEffectiveDailyExperience` | IL 1072 | `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` | true (39) |
| `transferred-save-troop-wage-guard` | `DefaultPartyWageModelInterface.GetTotalWage` | IL 887 | `GameInterface` | true (42) |
| `transferred-save-troop-xp-guard` | `PartyBase.OnXpChanged` | IL 159 | `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` | true (38) |
| `CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent` | `ObjectManager.Remove` IL (const :40) | `F043B6618363D7FD0117898EA3C61CAB8BFE38F35067A807C583CDF81BF875E3` | `GameInterface` (owner of `ObjectManager.Remove`) | true |
| `CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent` | routine-flush IL (const :36) | `365686E1E7FF439FB8E2C1C1DBD5191EB5D4DFC250F64A7D96B99D7A8E81A8F0` | not TAOM | true |
| `CatchUpDrainGuardComponent` | resume IL (const :33) | `8A553CD31A4882E29CF88CC744A5DF61009DF220F0AFD5CB2BBBBB219C6BFB3C` | not TAOM | true |

| `RaidLootIsolationComponent` | four seams, six accepted hashes (two seams accept either of two) | `3493A88E…`, `C8789537…`, `CEF0FA2B…`, `5DA68A5D…`, `D45E9807…`, `211B2271…` | not TAOM | true |

[Verified] None of the four hash components points at TAOM: between them they qualify `Coop.Core` `03ff70df-eadc-46c9-a51e-b68e8c3336ef`, `GameInterface` `3c2da906-f6ec-443d-afa8-aad1c8286c03` and `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` `886629fe-6e60-40d7-9a57-8d46017179d9`, so a TAOM rebuild does not move them.

**`troop-weight-policy` breaks first, and not on the MVID.** [Verified] `TroopWeightPolicyComponent` is `Critical => true` (24) and checks only `type.Assembly.FullName` and `type.FullName`, with no MVID in the file, so an MVID change alone does not trip it; its error text at line 145 still says `"does not belong to the exact TAOM 2.0.20 contract"`, a stale string from an earlier re-cut. But `Install` resolves four TAOM members by exact signature before patching, and `ResolveExactSizePenalty` requires `parameters.Length == 2` against the one public instance overload `public void ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty(PartyBase, ref ExplainedNumber)`. [Verified] That signature moved: `git diff v2.0.26..HEAD -- Main/Features/TroopWeight/TroopWeightService.cs` adds a third parameter `bool includeDescriptions`, and both `TAOM.dll` copies on this machine carry `ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty(PartyBase, ref ExplainedNumber, Boolean) : Void` read out of PE metadata this turn. [Verified] `troop-weight-policy` is registration #13 of 78, ahead of `siege-defense-telemetry` (#24) and `field-camp-coop-suppression` (#36). [Inference] So on today's build the first critical fault is not the MVID pin: `troop-weight-policy` throws `MissingMethodException` 65 registrations before `field-camp-coop-suppression` reads an MVID, and both TAOM-GUID components are skipped without running their checks. Two independent contracts are broken. [Verified] The coupling does not stop at `TAOM.dll`: `CoopPatchShieldPolicy.ShieldOwner` is the literal Harmony owner string `"TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield"` and `CoopPatchShieldComponent` resolves `TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.CoopPresence` by name, so two type names in `Dependencies/Foundation/` are load-bearing too.

### Three release cadences, not one

[Verified] Across both trees the components pin exactly 7 distinct GUIDs, only one of them TAOM's.

| `TAOM` | `bd1af67e-d002-47d1-bf58-83e8967fd143` | this repo | 4 |
| `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem` | `886629fe-6e60-40d7-9a57-8d46017179d9` | engine | 11 |
| `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade` (client) | `4267abec-e4ba-4746-94b7-47cbe3ba79f8` | engine | 2 |
| `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade` (server) | `1bcf228e-dc3e-4af3-8e86-8d749905f4c2` | engine | 2 |
| `GameInterface` | `3c2da906-f6ec-443d-afa8-aad1c8286c03` | BannerlordCoop | 17 |
| `Coop.Core` | `03ff70df-eadc-46c9-a51e-b68e8c3336ef` | BannerlordCoop | 4 |
| `Missions` | `66f75064-7cf2-4bd5-b83c-d286de3e2f77` | BannerlordCoop | 1 |

The engine half of the contract is intact today and the TAOM half is not. [Inference] A Steam force-bump breaks the compat through the same mechanism with no TAOM release involved.

[Verified] The Coop half is pinned harder and sits outside the fault machinery: `CoopDependencyBridgePolicy` holds a 7-entry `CoopDependencyPin[]`, each with an exact byte length, exact file SHA-256 and exact assembly full name (`protobuf-net.Core.dll` 308,512; `protobuf-net.dll` 296,224; `LiteNetLib.dll` 126,240; `Common.dll` 122,144; `GameInterface.dll` 4,513,568; `Missions.dll` 952,608; `Coop.Core.dll` 540,960). `QualifyFile` throws `InvalidDataException("Pinned Coop dependency does not match the Workshop v0.1.4 tuple: " + path)` on any mismatch, from `BootstrapSubModule`'s constructor, whose whole body is `CoopDependencyBridge.InstallAndPreload();`. [Inference] That throw never reaches `ComponentRegistry` and never calls `MarkFaulted`, so a CoopNightly update fails outside every graceful path below.

### The numbers today

[Verified] Measured this turn with `PEReader` + `MetadataReader` and `Get-FileHash`:

| File | MVID | Bytes | SHA-256 | Written |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Main/_Module/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/TAOM.dll` (repo build output) | `7c21ec91-b4a6-417e-8e00-bbe122269b0c` | 3,104,768 | `D5D62E0D3209…` | 2026-09-07 09:41:44 |
| `C:\Program Files (x86)\Steam\steamapps\common\Mount & Blade II Bannerlord\Modules\TAOM\bin\Win64_Shipping_Client\TAOM.dll` | `696fdc47-85c2-4931-9919-f88cdcb00b89` | 3,102,720 | `8BF77112619A…` | 2026-09-07 09:10:14 |

Three GUIDs, no two alike. The two local copies were written 31 minutes 30 seconds apart and differ by 2,048 bytes and by hash, so they are separate compilations and neither confirms nor refutes MVID reproducibility. (`E:\Steam\...` does not exist here; the `C:\Program Files (x86)` path is this machine's install, an environment observation, not a repo defect.) [Verified] Both DLLs report `AssemblyName=TAOM, Version=2.0.0.0`, which is why assembly identity cannot separate releases: `Main/TAOM.csproj:4` fixes `<Version>2.0.0</Version>` while `Main/_Module/SubModule.xml:6` carries `<Version value="v2.0.28" />`. [Inference] The MVID is the only per-build discriminator a Harmony patch can read at runtime, so the technique is right for the guarantee wanted; the cost is build granularity, not release granularity. [Verified] Repo drift on branch `bannerlord-coop`: `git describe --tags --abbrev=0` returns `v2.0.28`; `git rev-list --count v2.0.26..HEAD` returns **35**, **26** of them since `v2.0.28`. `HEAD` is `1e561a95 feat: Implement Patch84 to guard against null _besiegerParty in siege aftermath menus`. Tags resolve to `badf9086` (`v2.0.26`, 2026-09-02 11:52:23), `d689f297` (`v2.0.27`, 2026-09-04 12:14:04), `8ce42fe8` (`v2.0.28`, 2026-09-04 13:04:14). Since `v2.0.26`, **153** of **741** changed files are under `Main/**/*.cs`.

[Verified] Neither pinned TAOM seam moved. `git diff --stat v2.0.26..HEAD` is empty for `Main/Features/FieldCamp/` and `Main/Adapters/MapEventBattleLogAdapter.cs`, and reading IL out of both `TAOM.dll` copies this turn gives `TAOM.Features.FieldCamp.UI.FieldCampOverlayVM.Refresh` at **295** bytes and `ExecuteOpenCampMenu` at **54** in each, matching the constants. (`MapEventBattleLogAdapter.SnapshotStart` reads **297** bytes in both; the compat pins that method's signature and MVID, not its length.) The IL gate would pass on either binary and never runs, because the MVID moved anyway, carried by 153 unrelated files. [Verified] The compat's strings record four earlier rounds of this: `2.0.16` (`SettingsFingerprintComponent.cs:28`), `2.0.20` (`TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs:145`, `MonsterRegistrationComponent.cs:193`, `AuthoredMonsterContract.cs:26`, `FaceGenComponent.cs:258`), `2.0.23` (`TransferredSaveTroopRosterMigrationComponent.cs:236`), `2.0.26` (`FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent.cs:48`, `:79`, `:89`, plus `Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs:9`).

### What a miss costs

[Verified] `InstallAll` catches the throw (94 to 103), logs `install FAILED`, runs `TryUnpatch` and `TryDeactivate`, and calls `CompatState.MarkFaulted("critical component \"<name>\" failed to install")` when `SafeCritical`. Every remaining component is then skipped at line 65 with `" | NOT installed: the module already faulted earlier in this pass"`, and after the loop `RollBackAll("module faulted during install")` (105 to 107) unpatches and deactivates everything already live. [Verified] `SafeCritical` (210) returns `true` from its own `catch`, so the failure mode defaults to fatal. [Verified] Of the 83 files under `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/` referencing `ICompatComponent`, 25 are `Critical => true` and 58 `Critical => false`; 78 components are registered. [Verified] At `OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot`, `_registry.OnGameFrameworkReady()` aborts on the first failing component (`"registry | framework-ready pass ABORTED"`) and rolls back again.

[Verified] `CompatState` is a one-way latch, `Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref _faulted, 1, 0)`, reset only by the public `Reset()` (which also calls `ServerAttestation.ResetForTests()`). **Its log line overstates what the code enforces:** `"FAULTED | " + Reason + " | hosting, joining and campaign-ready are blocked for the rest of this process"`, but only two enforcement paths exist. `JoinRefusal.Install` prefixes `BoundaryMethods` (`"StartAsClient"`, `"StartAsServer"`) on `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience` with `RefuseWhileFaulted`, returning `false` while faulted and announcing through `TaleWorlds.Library.InformationManager` resolved by name; it **fails open**, its `catch` logging `"join-refusal | check threw (...); allowing the session"` and returning `true`. [Verified] `Install` returns `false` if the type or a boundary method is missing, and `OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot` then faults, rolls back and throws rather than continue unguarded.

[Verified] The second path, `CoopCompatSubModule.EnforceFaultAtCampaignStart` (582, called from `OnGameStart` at 495), self-describes as `"the independent enforcement path; the session-boundary guard either did not install or was bypassed"` and is **conditional**: past an unconditional `JoinRefusal.RestartRequired` branch its guard is `if (!CompatState.Faulted || CoopRole.SessionActive() == false) { return; }`. `CoopRole.SessionActive()` returns `bool?`, reflecting into `GameInterface.ContainerProvider.TryGetContainer` and answering `null` when unreachable. [Inference] With Coop installed and no session live the probe answers `false`, so a faulted module still permits a campaign start; the campaign-start hard stop bites only when a session is live or the probe cannot answer.

[Verified] `RestartRequired` is reached through one of four `TearDownFaultedSession` outcomes, run from `OnApplicationTick` on a 1 second retry countdown: `NotNeeded` returns; **`Succeeded`** is the only branch calling `JoinRefusal.MarkRestartRequired()`; `TerminallyUnsafe` throws immediately (`"failed part way through … The network may still be live with the container already released"`); an unconfirmed teardown increments a counter and throws at `MaxTeardownAttempts = 5` (21). Either throw propagates out of the tick, where the SubModule logs `"session teardown | TERMINAL - refusing to continue"` and rethrows. Once `RestartRequired` is set both paths are unconditional. [Self-report, not independently verified] The stated reason, that Coop's `IGameInterface.UnpatchAll` is a no-op in this build so its patches stay attached to live game methods, exists only inside the compat's own `CompatLog.Error` string in the `Succeeded` branch; no Coop assembly is installed here to check it. [Verified] `ServerAttestation.RegisterServingFaultHardStop` logs that `"the wrapper aborts the process on a CompatState fault before any Serving work"`, and `TearDownFaultedSession` early-returns when `ServingFaultHardStopRegistered` is set: a dedicated server aborts rather than refusing. [Verified] `CompatLog` writes to a file only, `coopcompat-<pid>.log` under a resolved log directory, with `Info`/`Warn`/`Error` all funnelling into `File.AppendAllText`; nothing reaches the screen at install time. The only in-game message is `JoinRefusal.Announce` from `RefuseWhileFaulted`: `"TAOM Coop Compatibility is faulted and has refused the session: ..."`, seen when the player clicks host or join. So a boot-time miss stops hosting and joining at `StartAsServer` and `StartAsClient` for the life of the process, while single-player keeps running with every compat patch rolled back and campaigns still starting.

**Not reproducible by default, which makes the treadmill worse than a version pin.** [Verified] `Main/TAOM.csproj:5-6` and `Dependencies/TAOM.Dependencies.csproj:5-6` both set `<Deterministic>true</Deterministic>` and `<PathMap>$(MSBuildProjectDirectory)=/_/</PathMap>`, so Roslyn hashes the compilation inputs instead of emitting a random GUID. That is not enough, because TAOM deliberately puts the clock into the inputs. [Verified] `Directory.Build.props:23` defines `<TaomBuildStamp Condition="'$(TaomBuildStamp)' == ''">$([System.DateTime]::UtcNow.ToString('yyyyMMdd-HHmmss'))Z</TaomBuildStamp>`, and `:24` feeds it into `<InformationalVersion>build.$(TaomBuildStamp)</InformationalVersion>`. It reaches the binary: `Main/obj/Debug/net472/TAOM.AssemblyInfo.cs` emits `[assembly: AssemblyInformationalVersionAttribute("build.20260907-145943Z+1e561a958d5601a94eb6f990263835a9549c8663")]`, and a `PEReader` read of the shipped `Main/_Module/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/TAOM.dll` returns that exact string. An assembly attribute is a compilation input, so a second-resolution timestamp moves the MVID on **every** build, including a rebuild of an unchanged tree. [Verified] Four distinct MVIDs were measured off this repo inside one working day: `40f9ed03` (09-06 23:13), `7c21ec91` (09-07 09:41), `b5f89f5b` (10:46) and `b6786d7a` (10:59), none of them the pinned `bd1af67e`. **The escape hatch already exists and is documented three lines above the property:** `Directory.Build.props:20` says "Set TaomBuildStamp explicitly for a reproducible build". A release that pins `TaomBuildStamp` to the tag rather than `UtcNow` would make the shipped binary reproducible from its commit, which is the one thing that would let a compat author re-derive a pin instead of receiving it. The MVID is still a function of the whole compilation:

- **TAOM cannot ship any release that keeps co-op working.** Not just breaking changes: any change. [Verified] `v2.0.27` and `v2.0.28` are both tagged. [Inference] Each built a new `TAOM.dll`, and the first put the v2.0.26 pin out of contract.
- **The re-cut is on the compat side and needs the new binary:** read its MVID, re-measure the pinned IL lengths, edit the constants, rebuild, redistribute. [Verified] `coop-taom.json` rejects any top-level key outside `schemaVersion`, `protocolVersion` and `components`, so the expected MVID cannot come from config. [Inference] That leaves a source edit and a compat release per TAOM release; the evidence is a grep for the literal GUID and for `ModuleVersionId`, so a config path storing it under another identifier would not have shown up.
- **Signature pins break independently.** [Verified] `TroopWeightPolicyComponent` already fails on the current build for a non-MVID reason, so refreshing GUIDs and IL lengths alone is not enough.
- **Players get the failure late and quietly**, triggered by a routine TAOM update, with nothing on screen until they try to host or join.

**Almost no signal exists on the TAOM side.** [Verified] `git grep -li "coopcompat"` over every tracked file returns zero. [Verified] `.github/workflows/build.yml` is the only workflow, 278 lines and 13 named steps, and a case-insensitive grep of it for `coop`, `mvid` and `moduleversionid` returns nothing. [Verified] `git grep -li "moduleversionid\|mvid"` matches 13 tracked files: one source file (`Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:312`, where it is the dictionary key `$"{originalMethod.Module.ModuleVersionId}:{originalMethod.MetadataToken}"`, not a gate), three vendored DLLs under `Dependencies/_Module/bin/Win64_Shipping_Client/`, five binary assets and four historical docs. None is a gate. [Verified] The repo is not co-op unaware: `Main/Features/CoopInterop/` ships `ICoopPresenceProvider`, `ICoopSessionProvider` and `IDedicatedServerProvider` with implementations, documented in `docs/features/coop-interop.md` plus a CLAUDE.md trap row. TAOM knows at runtime whether a co-op mod is present; what it knows nothing about, at build or release time, is the binary contract another module froze against its assembly. [Inference] The treadmill is invisible from inside TAOM's release process (bump, tag, push, all green while the compat is already out of contract), and the cheapest closure is a release-time step recording the shipped `TAOM.dll` MVID and the two pinned seam IL lengths in the release notes. That is a suggestion, not current practice; I did not read `/release` or `docs/reference/release-process.md` this turn.

## Suppression versus replication: two projects, opposite jobs

Both codebases exist because BannerlordCoop leaves the global campaign tick running on every peer. TAOM asks what it must stop doing so two campaigns do not diverge; `TAOM.CoopCompat` asks how to make the stopped thing work across peers anyway. Read either one with the other's question in mind and it looks incoherent.

### TAOM: three stand-down signals

`Main/Features/CoopInterop/` is 17 top-level `.cs` files plus a `Diagnostics/` folder holding exactly three (`HarmonyCensusModels.cs`, `HarmonyCensusReportBuilder.cs`, `HarmonyCensusWriter.cs`, none a provider). No `System.Net`, socket, or send API appears in any of the 20 files [Verified]. The whole product is **three interfaces carrying six boolean members** (`ICoopPresenceProvider.IsCoopActive`; `ICoopSessionProvider.IsSessionActive` / `IsAuthority` / `IsCoopClient` / `ShouldDeferToHost` at `ICoopSessionProvider.cs:21,27,30,42`; `IDedicatedServerProvider.IsDedicatedServer`), kept apart because each answers a different question with a different lifetime and a different safe failure [Verified].

| Signal | Detection | Varies in-process? | Fail direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ICoopPresenceProvider.IsCoopActive` | `CoopPresence.IsActive` reflects the launcher's active-module id list via `IncompatibleModDetector.TryReadActiveModuleIdsViaReflection()`, matched by exact `OrdinalIgnoreCase` `HashSet.Contains` against `CompiledModuleDefaults` (`BannerlordTogether`, `BattleLinkMPClient`, `Coop`) unioned with a shipped `coop-modules.txt` that can add ids but never remove one | No, process-constant. Its own doc comment calls this the only co-op fact safe to read near a Harmony patch-application site | **Closed.** An empty active-module list means unknown, not none: `CoopPresencePolicy.ResolveActiveIds` returns nothing and TAOM behaves as unmodded. A `coop-force-active.flag` supplies a `(forced-by-flag)` marker only when detection found nothing, so it can add presence but never mask a real detection |
| `ICoopSessionProvider.IsAuthority` / `ShouldDeferToHost` (2 of 4 members) | `CoopSessionProvider` binds `GameInterface.ContainerProvider.TryGetContainer(out ILifetimeScope)` and static `Common.ModInformation.IsServer` by reflection, with no compile-time Coop reference. Binding cached under `_bindLock`; invocation repeated per call because the answer moves | Yes, session-varying | **Open, to singleplayer.** `IsAuthority(sessionActive, isServer) => !sessionActive \|\| isServer`, so any unreadable probe reports authoritative. If `TryGetContainer` binds but `IsServer` does not, `BindLocked` nulls the method out on purpose rather than let a default-false flag turn every peer into a client |
| `IDedicatedServerProvider.IsDedicatedServer` | `Assembly.GetExecutingAssembly().Location` searched for `Win64_Shipping_Server` (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`), wrapped in a `Lazy<bool>` | No, process-constant | **Closed**, to "not a server", because every gate built on it only suppresses |

Two traps are pinned in source doc comments rather than in a doc: `ContainerProvider.Alive` is an inline static initialiser evaluated while its backing field is still null, so it is permanently false and must never be the session probe; `ModInformation.IsServer` defaults false, is sticky across teardown, and has `IsClient` as its negation, so read alone it reports "client" in plain singleplayer whenever the module is merely enabled [Verified]. `ShouldDeferToHost` reads `CoopPresence.IsActive` directly instead of going through `Probe()`, because an active-but-unprobeable co-op mod must yield on every peer rather than fall through to "no session, therefore solo" [Verified].

**Reach.** 67 `.cs` files under `Main/` mention "coop" case-insensitively, 47 of them outside `Features/CoopInterop/`. 38 name one of the three interfaces, across 14 feature folders plus `Main/SubModule.cs`; the concentration is `Enlistment` (14 files) and `Diplomacy` (6) [Verified].

### CoopCompat: replication and arbitration

`TAOM.CoopCompat` `AssemblyVersion` 0.3.15.0, informational version `0.3.15-taom-2.0.26-authoritative-party-settings` [Verified]. Its shape is the inverse of TAOM's in every structural respect:

- **100 `.cs` files in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/`, 144 in `.Core/`, 50 in `.Relay/`.** `CoopCompatSubModule.cs` carries **78 `_registry.Add(` lines with 78 distinct arguments** (10 constructed inline, 68 pre-built fields), but the Components folder holds **83 classes implementing `ICompatComponent`**. Five compile in and are referenced nowhere outside their own file: `ArmyRegistrationRepairComponent`, `BattleSizeCeilingComponent`, `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent`, `BattleUplinkGuardComponent`, `LocationNpcEquipmentSamplingGuardComponent` [Verified].
- **It declares types inside Coop's own namespaces:** 11 files in `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat`, 6 in `Coop.Core.Server.Services.TaomCoopCompat`. **16 of those 17 are handlers** (`: IHandler, IDisposable`) subscribing on Coop's `IMessageBroker`; the 17th, `CareerClientEndpoint`, is an `internal static class` with no broker and no subscription. Contracts are ProtoBuf `Network*` types from `TAOM.CoopCompat.Relay` (26 contract files, 15 of them `NetworkTaom*`), and six handlers subscribe to **no** `NetworkTaom*` type at all: `AuthoritativePositionClientHandler`, `BattleSpawnReliabilityClientHandler`, `CareerAuthorityClientHandler`, `CompressedHeroJoinTransferClientHandler`, `WotrRelayClientHandler`, `CareerAuthorityServerHandler`. Several also subscribe to Coop's own lifecycle messages (`CampaignReady`, `CharacterCreationFinished`, `BattleMissionReady`, `MissionPeerLeft`, `MissionPeerDisconnected`) [Verified].
- **It takes compile-time references in both directions, though not everywhere.** `SpecialResourceAuthorityComponent` has `using TAOM.Features.SpecialResources;`; `CareerAuthorityComponent` has `using TAOM.Features.CareerSystem;` and `using Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat;` [Verified]. TAOM's `CoopSessionProvider` refuses exactly this on purpose. The momentum path is the counterexample: `TaomMomentumSeam` reaches TAOM through `AccessTools.TypeByName("TAOM.IoC")` and a generic `Resolve<T>` over `TAOM.Features.WarOfTheRingMomentum.IMomentumStateStore`, with no compile-time reference at all [Verified].
- **It pins Coop's IL and MVID, not just its names.** `CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent` carries `ExpectedRoutineFlushIlSha256 = "365686E1E7FF439FB8E2C1C1DBD5191EB5D4DFC250F64A7D96B99D7A8E81A8F0"` plus `ExpectedRosterRemovalIlSha256 = "F043B6618363D7FD0117898EA3C61CAB8BFE38F35067A807C583CDF81BF875E3"`, and throws `InvalidOperationException` (naming the assembly and its `ModuleVersionId`) when the loaded `Coop.Core` is not the qualified v0.1.4 contract [Verified]. TAOM's provider fails quietly to singleplayer where CoopCompat refuses to start.

It re-implements TAOM's authority predicate for its own use: `CoopRole.MayMutateSimulation(out Role role)` returns true only when `!CompatState.Faulted && role == Role.Server && SessionActive() == true` [Verified]. That fails **closed** to "may not mutate", the opposite of `CoopSessionPolicy.IsAuthority` [Verified]. Both are correct for their own jobs and are not interchangeable [Inference].

### What CoopCompat attacks in TAOM's own gap list

`docs/features/coop-interop.md` lists five items under **What is NOT done**. Four of the five have a named component pointed at them.

| Gap in `coop-interop.md` | What attacks it | State |
|---|---|---|
| **No MCM settings parity: reported, not exchanged.** TAOM computes a fingerprint over 175 simulation-relevant settings of 234, but peers never trade it | Two things, one working. `SettingsFingerprintComponent` (`DefaultMode = Observe`) resolves two assembly-qualified types, `TAOM.Features.CoopInterop.SettingsFingerprintLog` (shape-verifying `Write` and `WriteAcross`) and `TAOM.Features.CoopInterop.SettingsFingerprint` (`ComputeAcross`), then logs that invocation is `"pending TAOM logger/settings wiring (port task)"`. `TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent` sidesteps parity: it Harmony-prefixes the **39 TAOM settings getters** in its `Keys` map by name (`TAOM.Features.Diplomacy.TaomSettingsProvider.Phase1TriggerDay`, `…BattleBalanceSettingsProvider.Tier7Power`, and so on), plus a 40th patch on `TaomSettingsProvider.IsAvailable` via `OverrideAvailability`, plus party size and battle size through `TaomPartySizeSettingsAdapter` / `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter`. A client getter then returns the server value from `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy`'s **46 `ServerSettingDefinition` entries** (`SchemaVersion = 1`, `TaomProtocolVersion = 3`, `FileName = "taom-coop-settings.json"`), which are the 39 `Keys` plus the 7 `party.*` keys the party-size adapter reads. Battle size is not one of them: it rides alongside as `BattleSizeAuthorityPolicy.ServerDefault()` on the snapshot | Superseded, not solved. Divergence stops mattering for the 46 server-owned keys; the fingerprint half is shape-verified and unwired [Verified] |
| **No ModuleData content hash** | **Nothing.** SHA-256 appears in nine files, all for other jobs: IL pins (`CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent`, `CatchUpDrainGuardComponent`, `HeroHomeSettlementDiagnosticCoalescerComponent`, `RaidLootIsolationComponent.RequireIlHash`), a 12-hex log tag (`KingdomDecisionProbeComponent.Sha12`), career claim receipt filenames (`CareerClaimReceiptStore`), deterministic battle scene seeding, `PeerIdentity.SelfHash()`, the settings endpoint. The only `ModuleData` path in the assembly is `ArmoryRaceTable.cs:32` reading `ModuleData/skins.xml`. `LegacyModuleConflictComponent` throws rather than boot alongside four retired ids (`DedicatedServer.CoopWotrRelay`, `DedicatedServer.WotrRelay`, `DedicatedServer.CoopVoiceCache`, `DedicatedServer.TAOMFaceGen`), a module-identity check, not a content hash | Untouched [Verified] |
| **No sync of TAOM's own campaign state** (WotR momentum, culture-conversion deltas) | Half. `WotrMomentumRelayComponent` plus `WotrMomentumRelayBehavior` broadcast `NetworkWotrMomentumState` via `INetwork.SendAll` on `CampaignEvents.HourlyTickEvent` under `CoopRole.MayMutateSimulation`, throttled to one send per 60 real seconds with a 12-hour keep-alive, carrying a `Dictionary<string, string>` from TAOM's own `IMomentumStateStore.Serialize` (reached through `TaomMomentumSeam`) and applied client-side through `Deserialize`. `WotrRelayClientHandler` is the subscriber. The string `CultureConversion` appears nowhere in the assembly | Momentum closed, culture conversion untouched [Verified] |
| **WotR participation credits one kingdom, not one session** | Nothing aimed at the crediting rule. The relay ships the server's already-computed store whole, and `momentum.requirePlayerParticipation`, `momentum.minimumPlayerEvents`, `momentum.participationMultiplier` are three of the 46 server-owned keys, making the rule uniform across peers | Untouched. Uniform inputs to a test that still resolves against one local player kingdom is not per-session crediting [Verified for the mechanism, Inference for the conclusion: `WarEventSnapshotAdapter` / `IsPlayerRelated` was not read this turn] |
| **Dedicated server: no longer "unsupported", but not supported either** | Six registered components: `server-boot-fault-blocker` (`Critical => true`, throwing `"server boot fault blocking has no safe observe mode"` rather than offer one), `server-log-sink`, `server-warning-dump-suppression`, `facegen` with `TaomHeadlessFaceGen`, `monster-registration`, `server-gameplay-settings`. `ServerAttestation` is the readiness state they gate on, read by `FaceGenComponent`, `MonsterRegistrationComponent`, `ServerBootFaultBlockerComponent`, `ServerLogSinkComponent`, `CompatState`, `CoopCompatSubModule` (not by `server-gameplay-settings` or `server-warning-dump-suppression`). `SpecialResourceAuthorityComponent` attacks the exact 2026-08-03 finding, logging on arm: "server earns/ticks special resources for player heroes and pushes rows; clients apply rows and report measured spend deltas" | Actively worked. The taxonomy narrows "not supported either": `ComponentMode` is a three-value enum (`Off`, `Observe`, `Enabled`) with `Critical` a separate `bool` on `ICompatComponent` [Verified] |

The sharpest illustration of the inversion is not on that list. TAOM's verification checklist item 9 asks for "an emissary purchase attempted on a guest: it must decline, and no resources may be deducted", which is suppression working correctly. `EmissaryPurchaseProtocolComponent` logs "TAOM purchase boundary replaced by authenticated server request/result v2 for host and guests", builds a `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseRequestV2`, and `EmissaryPurchaseClientEndpoint` sends it; `EmissaryPurchaseServerHandler` subscribes and replies with `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseResultV2` [Verified]. The guest no longer declines; the guest asks.

### The evidence base under all of this is stale

`coop-interop.md`'s field-report section states its provenance: on 2026-08-03 a testing group ran **TAOM v2.0.16 on Bannerlord 1.4.7.117484 across three configurations** (BannerlordCoop v0.1.1 client-hosted, a BannerlordCoop dedicated server, Bannerlord Together a0.5.3.1) [Verified]. CoopCompat 0.3.15 qualifies its seams against **Coop v0.1.3 and v0.1.4** by name, MVID and IL SHA-256, and names TAOM 2.0.26 in its own version string; `Main/_Module/SubModule.xml` is at `<Version value="v2.0.28" />` [Verified]. So the document defining what is not done was written against a Coop build two patch versions older than the oldest contract the compat pins, and a TAOM build 12 versions behind the repo. Read every "not done" item as a claim about August, not about the current pair [Inference]. That is a maintenance debt on TAOM's doc, and it makes the gap list the wrong artifact to hand a player asking what works today.

**One correction owed inside `coop-interop.md` itself.** The 234 / 175 totals are real and test-pinned: `TAOM.Tests/Features/CoopInterop/SettingsFingerprintTests.cs:210-213` asserts `TaomSettings` 220 reflected / 175 covered, `BattleLoadDiagnosticsSettings` 7/0, `BlowDiagnosticsSettings` 1/0, `CrashReportSettings` 6/0, summing to 234 and 175 [Verified]. The doc's parenthetical split does not: it says "211 in `TaomSettings`" against the test's 220, and 211+7+6+1 is 225, not 234. Its "57 excluded" is likewise off, since 234 minus 175 is 59. Neither figure is guarded, because `EveryDocQuotingTheSettingsCounts_AgreesWithReflection` only greps for the `**234**` and `**175 are simulation-relevant**` strings [Verified].

## Architecture: bootstrap, components, fault latch

TAOM.CoopCompat v0.3.15 ships as one Bannerlord module holding two managed assemblies; the split exists so assembly resolution is rigged before the CLR sees any type reference into Coop's binaries.

### Manifest and version pins

- [Verified] `Payload/Modules/TAOM.CoopCompat/SubModule.xml`: `Name`=`TAOM Coop Compatibility`, `Id`=`TAOM.CoopCompat`, `Version`=`v0.3.15`, `SingleplayerModule`=`true`, `MultiplayerModule`=`false`, `Xmls` an empty element (`<Xmls />`), `Tags` **absent entirely**.
- [Verified] `DependedModules`, five hard ids in order: `Native`, `TAOM.Dependencies`, `TAOM`, `LOTRLOME_Armory`, `CoopNightly`. The comment above the block: no `DedicatedServer.` prefix, because this module patches simulation so Coop's exact-version validation must include it on every machine.
- [Verified] `DependedModuleMetadatas`, six entries, all `order="LoadBeforeThis"`: `TAOM` `v2.0.23`, `TAOM.Dependencies` `v2.0.23`, `CoopNightly` `v0.1.4`, `LOTRLOME_Armory` (no version), none optional; plus the two optional ones, `Alliance.Wargs` and `TAOM_Map` (no version), which appear here but not in `DependedModules`.
- **Version skew, confirmed.** [Verified] The manifest pins `TAOM` / `TAOM.Dependencies` at `v2.0.23`, but the compat assembly's `AssemblyInformationalVersion` is `0.3.15-taom-2.0.26-authoritative-party-settings`. Three TAOM revisions are hard-pinned in code at once:

| Revision | Site | Pin shape |
|---|---|---|
| `2.0.20` | `AuthoredMonsterContract.cs:26`, `FaceGenComponent.cs:258` (race table), `MonsterRegistrationComponent.cs:193`, `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs:145` | throws on mismatch |
| `2.0.23` | `TransferredSaveTroopRosterMigrationComponent.cs:236` (retired-troop aliases) | `CompatLog.Info` only, no throw |
| `2.0.26` | `FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent.cs:79` and `:89` | throws on mismatch |

- [Verified] The 2.0.26 pin is not a version-string comparison. `FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent.RequireFrozenTaomAssembly()` compares `typeof(FieldCampOverlayVM).Assembly.FullName` to the literal `TAOM, Version=2.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=null` and the manifest module MVID to `bd1af67e-d002-47d1-bf58-83e8967fd143`, then `RequireTarget` pins IL body lengths of **295 bytes** (`FieldCampOverlayVM.Refresh`) and **54 bytes** (`FieldCampOverlayVM.ExecuteOpenCampMenu`). Failure text: `"TAOM FieldCamp overlay is outside the frozen 2.0.26 contract (assembly=..., mvid=...)"`; a seam-shape miss throws `MissingMethodException("exact TAOM 2.0.26 FieldCampOverlayVM.<name> seam was not found")`. It runs inside `Install` on a component declaring `Critical => true`, `DefaultMode => Enabled`.
- [Inference] TAOM's assembly version is `2.0.0.0` and does not move between module releases, so the MVID is the discriminator: any TAOM build but the exact one this compat build was cut against fails, whatever the manifest says, and a player on TAOM v2.0.23 satisfies the manifest yet still trips the throw. I did not read Bannerlord's `DependedModuleMetadata` version-comparison semantics this turn, so no claim on whether manifest `version=` is an exact match or a floor.
- [Verified] A fourth TAOM version string is not a pin: `SettingsFingerprintComponent.cs:28` logs the warning `seam absent (expected on TAOM 2.0.16; unexpected on 2.0.20)`.

### Bootstrap ordering and the dependency bridge

- [Verified] Two `<SubModule>` entries, in order: (1) `TAOM.CoopCompat Bootstrap` / `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` / `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.BootstrapSubModule`, commented `MUST remain first. Its constructor establishes exact Coop dependency bindings before Bannerlord scans the following assembly's TypeDefs.`; (2) `TAOM.CoopCompat` / `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` / `TAOM.CoopCompat.CoopCompatSubModule`, commented that it deliberately carries NO Tags: IL-verified 2026-08-10, the client loader accepts only `IsNoRenderModeElement="false"` and the no-render server loader only `"true"`, so no single value loads on both sides, while an EMPTY tag list short-circuits to loadable everywhere.
- [Verified] `BootstrapSubModule` overrides nothing; its entire body is a constructor calling `CoopDependencyBridge.InstallAndPreload()`. [Verified] `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` carries compile-time references to `Common`, `GameInterface`, `Missions`, `LiteNetLib`, `protobuf-net.Core` (alongside `TAOM`, `Serilog`, `0Harmony`, `System.Core`, `System.Xml` and five `TaleWorlds.*`), but **not** `Coop.Core`: every `Coop.Core.*` type is reached at runtime through `AccessTools.TypeByName` (`Coop.Core.Server.CoopServer`, `Coop.Core.Server.Services.Time.OverloadedPeerManager`, `Coop.Core.Common.Network.CoopNetworkBase`, `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience`), and the `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat` / `Coop.Core.Server.Services.TaomCoopCompat` namespaces are defined inside the compat assembly. The bootstrap's own csproj references only `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade`, `TaleWorlds.ModuleManager`, `System.Core`, so it has nothing of Coop's to resolve itself.
- [Inference] `OnSubModuleLoad` runs after the engine has instantiated every submodule type, by which point `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` is loaded and its TypeRefs must already resolve; doing the work in the first entry's constructor puts the `AssemblyResolve` handler in place while only the bootstrap assembly is loaded. I did not read the TaleWorlds module loader this turn.
- [Verified] Both role bins ship byte-identical binaries: `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` 14,336 bytes, sha256 `6eb31bcb677b924054d31ac8e1c10f6083309f81e37031faba26b535e79442e3`; `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` 788,992 bytes, sha256 `86acfbc45af5928d66fcd91aa0fbd274bf998e1ce10438e5fecc2cd0d5af53a4`. Hashed from both `Win64_Shipping_Client` and `Win64_Shipping_Server`, matching `CHECKSUMS.sha256` and `PACKAGE.json` `payloadFiles`. [Inference] Role is never a build flavour, only a runtime discovery.
- [Verified] `InstallAndPreload()`, under a static `lock (Sync)` with an `_installed` idempotency guard. (1) `ModuleHelper.GetModuleInfo("CoopNightly")` must be non-null, `IsActive` and ordinal-equal on `Id`, else `InvalidOperationException("TAOM.CoopCompat bootstrap requires the active Coop module.")`. (2) Role comes from the bin folder name of `typeof(CoopDependencyBridge).Assembly.Location` via `CoopDependencyBridgePolicy.TryGetRole`, accepted only if ordinal-equal to `Win64_Shipping_Client` or `Win64_Shipping_Server`, else a throw quoting the location.
- (3) `GetCanonicalRoleBin` builds `Path.GetFullPath(Combine(root, "bin", role))`, requires it to `StartsWith` the trimmed root plus `DirectorySeparatorChar` (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`) and to `Directory.Exists`; `GetCandidatePath` repeats containment per file and rejects any pin whose `FileName` is not ordinal-equal to its own `Path.GetFileName`, so there is no traversal through the pin table. (4) `QualifyFile` throws `FileNotFoundException` on a missing path, then requires all three of `FileInfo.Length == pin.Bytes`, `GetSha256(path)` ordinal-equal `pin.Sha256` (`BitConverter.ToString` uppercase hex, dashes stripped) and `AssemblyName.GetAssemblyName(path).FullName` ordinal-equal `pin.FullName`, else `InvalidDataException("Pinned Coop dependency does not match the Workshop v0.1.4 tuple: " + path)`.
- (5) `AppDomain.CurrentDomain.AssemblyResolve += Resolver` only after all seven candidates qualify. (6) `EnsureCanonicalLoaded` calls `GetLoadedExact` first, which throws `"Multiple assemblies are loaded under pinned Coop name '<name>'."` when more than one assembly answers that simple name, verifies a single existing one, and falls through to `LoadAndVerify` (second `QualifyFile`, `Assembly.LoadFrom(path)`, `VerifyLoadedAssembly`) only when nothing is loaded.
- (7) `VerifyUniqueLoaded` re-enumerates `AppDomain.CurrentDomain.GetAssemblies()` after each load and requires exactly one reference-equal match, re-running identity and file verification, else `"The canonical Coop dependency did not bind uniquely: " + pin.SimpleName`. (8) Any throw in the preload block does `AssemblyResolve -= Resolver`, `CanonicalPaths.Clear()` and rethrows, so no half-installed bridge survives; step 4 failures precede handler attachment. Success prints `[TAOM.CoopCompat] early Coop dependency bridge ready (<role>, <N> exact assemblies)` to `Console`.
- [Verified] `VerifyLoadedAssembly` rejects an assembly that is null, `IsDynamic`, has a blank `Location`, whose full path differs from expected (`Path.GetFullPath`, `OrdinalIgnoreCase`), or fails `IsExpectedIdentity`, then re-runs `QualifyFile` on `assembly.Location`. `IsExpectedIdentity` requires `requested.Name` to match `pin.SimpleName` (ignore case), `candidate.FullName` ordinal-equal `pin.FullName`, and `requested.Version == candidate.Version` when the request carried a version; culture and public key token ride inside the `FullName` compare. The resolve handler is deliberately non-throwing: unknown simple names return `null` (falling through to normal CLR probing), and any exception logs `[TAOM.CoopCompat] early dependency resolution failed for '<name>': <ex>` and returns `null`.

[Verified] All seven rows of `CoopDependencyBridgePolicy.Pins` (a `CoopDependencyPin[7]`, exposed as `CanonicalPins`), culture `neutral` on every row; the file name is the simple name plus `.dll` in every case:

| Simple name | Bytes | SHA-256 | Version / PublicKeyToken |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `protobuf-net.Core` | 308,512 | `E2F0603689735F1F64233E961C9AF00A9682724742EE685FAAA1F66C2CB855D5` | 3.0.0.0 / `257b51d87d2e4d67` |
| `protobuf-net` | 296,224 | `654998ECE41700293233BBC776A08B5B072959CE6BFEA1098EB1E2ADAB3F728F` | 3.0.0.0 / `257b51d87d2e4d67` |
| `LiteNetLib` | 126,240 | `6D4114FFBE5FB1F818445B46D3F8AD1642285E388839BF47EE2DBA36E4E3A650` | 1.3.1.0 / null |
| `Common` | 122,144 | `D31409D82B93FBB2B5560AA48248EAA3BB93019E3AF9C580F8F383599C0EFDB7` | 1.0.0.0 / null |
| `GameInterface` | 4,513,568 | `FF1736FA6F76C7C1239A148112892C7B7F7FC6510701BFC5588DB377BDA76440` | 1.0.0.0 / null |
| `Missions` | 952,608 | `FA039E5AB1AD80BA51E56F8CB8E469034EEE99CC7F792ECFCD095D56387ADD82` | 1.0.0.0 / null |
| `Coop.Core` | 540,960 | `45924C8D5794D39EBF2A2E5E6206394837F25DFBFBD739A84C7163D191EABFB2` | 1.0.0.0 / null |

[Verified] Pinned bytes sum to 6,860,256. The pin set is not the compat assembly's reference set: `Coop.Core` and `protobuf-net` are pinned but are not compile-time references, `Serilog` and `0Harmony` are references but are not pinned. [Inference] This is a whole-file identity pin (length plus SHA-256 plus `AssemblyName.FullName`), so any recompile of CoopNightly, however trivial, fails `QualifyFile` and the module refuses to boot.

### Component model

- [Verified] `ICompatComponent` (in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Core`) is exactly five members: `string Name { get; }`, `ComponentMode DefaultMode { get; }`, `bool Critical { get; }`, `void Install(Harmony harmony, ComponentMode mode)`, `void OnGameFrameworkReady(ComponentMode mode)`. `ComponentMode` is `Off, Observe, Enabled`; `IDeactivatable` is a single `void Deactivate()`. **There is no abstract base class anywhere in the decompile**: every component implements all five directly, and 29 also implement `IDeactivatable` for non-Harmony teardown.
- [Verified] **83 classes** name `ICompatComponent` in a base list, all in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components`. 77 default to `Enabled`, 6 to `Observe`, none to `Off`; the six are `BattleFrameHitchProbeComponent`, `GhostReferenceSweepComponent`, `JoinBaselineRepairComponent`, `KingdomDecisionProbeComponent`, `SettingsFingerprintComponent`, `SiegeDefenseTelemetryComponent`. 25 declare `Critical => true`, 58 declare `false`.
- [Verified] `CoopCompatSubModule.OnSubModuleLoad` makes exactly **78 `_registry.Add(...)` calls** over 78 distinct types, so **five implementors are never registered**: `ArmyRegistrationRepairComponent`, `BattleSizeCeilingComponent`, `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent`, `BattleUplinkGuardComponent`, `LocationNpcEquipmentSamplingGuardComponent` (`grep -rl` finds each name only in its own file). Three of them declare `Critical => true`, leaving **22 critical components in the install list**. [Inference] "Dead" is scoped to this decompile; an external or future assembly could register them.
- [Verified] Per-component Harmony instance: `ComponentRegistry` declares `public const string HarmonyIdPrefix = "taom.coopcompat"` (declared, never referenced in the decompile) and constructs `new Harmony("taom.coopcompat." + component.Name)` from the literal, rolling back with `harmony.UnpatchAll(harmony.Id)` so a failing component never touches a sibling's patches. `JoinRefusal` gets its own instance with the hardcoded id `taom.coopcompat.join-refusal`, created in `CoopCompatSubModule`, not by the registry. `CoopPatchShieldComponent` is the exception to the model: a `public static class`, not an `ICompatComponent`, installed through `TryInstallEarly()` outside the registry.
- [Verified] `InstallAll()`: (1) group `SafeName` case-insensitively, and any group with count > 1 calls `CompatState.MarkFaulted("duplicate component names registered: ...")` and returns **without installing anything**; (2) `_config.ValidateComponents(names)`, log every warning, then if `!_config.IsValid` log every error, `MarkFaulted("configuration invalid — all components off")` and return.
- (3) Per component, skipping the rest of the pass once `CompatState.Faulted` (each skip logs `NOT installed: the module already faulted earlier in this pass`): read `Name`, resolve `ModeFor(name, DefaultMode)`; if `SafeCritical(component)` and the mode is not `Enabled`, `MarkFaulted($"critical component \"{name}\" must be enabled (requested {mode})")`. `SafeCritical` wraps the property in try/catch and **returns `true` on throw**, so a component whose `Critical` getter blows up counts as critical; `SafeName` degrades to `component.GetType().Name`. `Off` logs `off` and skips; otherwise construct the Harmony instance, call `Install`, record `LiveEntry { Mode, Harmony }`. On any exception: `TryUnpatch` then `TryDeactivate`, and if critical `MarkFaulted("critical component \"<name>\" failed to install")`; a rollback that itself throws marks `"<name>: partial patches could not be rolled back"` and a `Deactivate` that throws marks `"<name>: non-Harmony deactivation failed"` (state declared indeterminate, not assumed clean). (4) If faulted at the end, `RollBackAll("module faulted during install")` unwinds every live component. [Verified] `OnGameFrameworkReady()` walks the same list, aborts and rolls back at the top of the loop if already faulted, skips non-live components, and on a critical failure both marks faulted and `RollBackAll`s before breaking out.

### Config, role probing, fault latch

- [Verified] `CompatConfig.Load()` reads **`coop-taom.json`** from `ModuleRoot()` = `Path.GetFullPath(Combine(Path.GetDirectoryName(assemblyLocation), "..", ".."))`, which walks out of `bin/<role>/` to `Modules/TAOM.CoopCompat/` from either role bin. Both path helpers swallow exceptions and fall back to `"."` (`ModuleRoot`) and `null` (`BinDir`); a throw anywhere in `Load` becomes the error `config load failed: <Type>: <msg>`. `SupportedSchemaVersion = 1`. A **missing file is valid** (`ParseJson` returns immediately if `!File.Exists`, leaving defaults), and the v0.3.15 payload ships exactly five files (`SubModule.xml` plus two DLLs in each role bin), so there is no `coop-taom.json`, no `.flag` files, and the shipped configuration is entirely defaults.
- [Verified] Exactly **three permitted top-level keys**: `schemaVersion`, `protocolVersion`, `components`; anything else errors `coop-taom.json: unknown top-level key "X"`. `schemaVersion` must be present, integral and equal to 1; `protocolVersion` is optional but if present integral and equal to 1; `components` must be an object whose values are `enabled` / `observe` / `off` (compared after `ToLowerInvariant`), and keys differing only by case are an error.
- [Verified] **Any error forces everything off and faults the module**, by two independent mechanisms: `ModeFor` returns `ComponentMode.Off` unconditionally when `!IsValid`, and `InstallAll` refuses to enter the install loop at all and marks the module faulted. A typo'd kill switch is itself an error (`coop-taom.json: component "X" matches no registered component (typo'd kill switch?)`), so a misspelled disable never silently does nothing.
- [Verified] Legacy flags: `ScanLegacyFlags` globs `*-disabled.flag` in the module root and, if non-null and different (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`), in the bin directory. The component name is the filename minus `-disabled.flag`, passed through `LegacyFlagAliases` (`coopserverlog` to `server-log-sink`; `exspouses-guard` to `exspouses-guard`, an identity mapping). Each match sets `ComponentMode.Off` and is recorded in `LegacyFlagsSeen`. A flag naming an unregistered component downgrades from error to warning (`legacy flag "X" matches no registered component — ignored`) and the entry is dropped from `_modes`. `Describe()` appends `DEPRECATED flag files honoured: ... — move to coop-taom.json components block`, and returns `INVALID — all components forced off | <errors>` when invalid.
- [Verified] `CoopRole` is four static methods over cached reflection with no hard reference to Coop types. `Current()` reads `Common.ModInformation.IsServer` (`BindingFlags.Static | Public`) via `AccessTools.TypeByName`, returning `Role.Unknown` when the type or property is absent or the getter throws. `SessionActive()` invokes `GameInterface.ContainerProvider.TryGetContainer` (`Static | Public | NonPublic`) with a one-element `object[]` for the out parameter, returning `bool?` = `null` when absent or on throw. `NoCoopSession()` is `true` only when `SessionActive() == false` **and** `Current() != Role.Server`. `MayMutateSimulation(out Role role)` is `true` only when `!CompatState.Faulted` **and** `role == Role.Server` **and** `SessionActive() == true`. [Inference] The `bool?` is load-bearing: an unverifiable session state is never treated as "no session", so both composed predicates return `false` under uncertainty.
