- [Verified] `CompatState` is a process-wide latch on an `int` with `Volatile.Read` / `Interlocked.CompareExchange`. A null or whitespace reason becomes `"unspecified critical failure (caller supplied no reason)"`. The **first** reason wins (`CompareExchange(ref _reason, reason, null)`); later faults log `FAULTED (already) | follow-on failure: ...`, and the first transition logs `FAULTED | <reason> | hosting, joining and campaign-ready are blocked for the rest of this process`. There is no un-fault path outside `Reset()`, which also calls `ServerAttestation.ResetForTests()`.

### Lifecycle, in order

- [Verified] `CoopCompatSubModule` holds **68** component instances as `private readonly` fields plus a `ComponentRegistry`; the remaining 10 registrations are constructed inline inside the `Add` call.
- (1) **Constructor** calls `CoopPatchShieldComponent.TryInstallEarly()`. (2) **`OnSubModuleLoad`**: `CompatLog.Open()`; `TryInstallEarly()` again, then `LogStatus()`; log `PeerIdentity.BuildStamp()` (informational version, `protocol v1`, and the first 16 hex characters of the assembly's own SHA-256, lowercased, or `unhashable`); `CompatConfig.Load()` and log `Describe()`; build the registry; 78 `Add` calls in a fixed order beginning with `new LegacyModuleConflictComponent()`; `InstallAll()`. If not faulted **and** `_serverBootFaultBlocker`, `_faceGen` and `_monsterRegistration` are all live, call `ServerAttestation.MarkModuleBootstrapped()`. Any escape logs `boot FAILED`, marks faulted with `boot escape: <Type>: <msg>` and **nulls `_registry`**, which makes every later `IsLive` fan-out a no-op.
- (3) **`OnBeforeInitialModuleScreenSetAsRoot`** (once, guarded by `_frameworkReadyRan`): `TryInstallSessionGuard()` builds `new Harmony("taom.coopcompat.join-refusal")` and calls `JoinRefusal.Install`; on failure it marks faulted, `RollBackAll`s and **throws**, with a message naming `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience.StartAsClient/StartAsServer`. Then `_registry?.OnGameFrameworkReady()`; an escape here marks faulted (`framework-ready escape: <Type>`) and rolls back but does not rethrow.
- (4) **`OnGameStart`** runs `EnforceFaultAtCampaignStart()` **first**, before anything else, then for a `CampaignGameStarter` attaches five campaign behaviors when live: transferred-save troop roster migration, monster registration, WotR momentum relay, full-war hostile reconciliation, special-resource authority. A failure there marks faulted and rethrows.
- (5) **`OnApplicationTick(dt)`** stashes `dt` into `_lastTickSeconds`, calls `TearDownFaultedSession()` (a throw is logged `session teardown | TERMINAL - refusing to continue` and rethrown), ticks `_serverBootFaultBlocker` **without an `IsLive` gate**, then fans out to 22 components each behind its own null check plus `IsLive`, **five** of them additionally wrapped in try/catch: equipment diagnostic coalescer, battle frame-hitch probe, compressed hero join transfer, battle receiver spike hysteresis, encounter MapEvent recovery. (6) **`OnBeforeMissionBehaviorInitialize(mission)`** runs four components, each in its own try/catch so one failure cannot starve the next: battle order timing probe, formation cache guard, siege formation snapshot, agent mount state probe.

### Three independent fail-closed paths

- [Verified] **1. `JoinRefusal`**, a Harmony prefix at the session boundary. It patches `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience.StartAsClient` and `StartAsServer`, every non-abstract non-generic overload found by `Instance | Static | Public | NonPublic` reflection. `Install` returns `false` if the type is missing or any overload fails to attach, verifies attachment afterwards with `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` (`patch did not attach` counts as failure) and rolls back partial installs before returning false. The prefix `RefuseWhileFaulted` returns `false` (skipping the original) when `_restartRequired` or `CompatState.Faulted`, increments a refusal counter and announces through reflected `TaleWorlds.Library.InformationManager.DisplayMessage`. If the check itself throws it logs a warning and **allows** the session. [Inference] That is the fail-open point that matters, because it sits on the session boundary; it is not the only swallow in the design (`CoopDependencyBridge.ResolveCanonicalAssembly` returns `null` on any exception, `JoinRefusal.Announce` swallows everything, `IsGuardedBy` returns `false` on throw).
- [Verified] **2. `EnforceFaultAtCampaignStart`**, independent, at `OnGameStart`; its own log line reads `this is the independent enforcement path; the session-boundary guard either did not install or was bypassed`. It throws `InvalidOperationException` if `JoinRefusal.RestartRequired`, and otherwise throws when faulted unless `CoopRole.SessionActive() == false`, so an unverifiable (`null`) session state still throws.
- [Verified] **3. `TearDownFaultedSession`**, per tick in `OnApplicationTick`. It short-circuits when already torn down, not faulted, or `ServerAttestation.ServingFaultHardStopRegistered`; otherwise it retries `JoinRefusal.TearDownLiveSession()` at `TeardownRetrySeconds = 1f` intervals up to `MaxTeardownAttempts = 5`, then throws. `TearDownLiveSession` returns `NotNeeded` when `SessionActive() == false` and `Retry` when the probe is unavailable (it will not assume there is nothing to tear down), locates `Coop.CoopMod.Coop` by static field, and refuses to act unless that instance's `container` field is reference-equal to the container from `ContainerProvider.TryGetContainer` (`refusing to dispose it and claim the session was torn down`); it then calls `Dispose()` and confirms all three of `Running == false`, `container == null` and `SessionActive() == false` before reporting `Succeeded`. A `Dispose` that throws part way returns `TerminallyUnsafe`, propagating an exception out of the tick. `ReadRunning` returns `true` on any failure to read, and when `Running` is absent or non-boolean, so an unreadable state counts as still running.
- [Verified] A fourth gate sits alongside these: `ServerAttestation.ServingFaultHardStopRegistered` short-circuits `TearDownFaultedSession` entirely, and `ServerBootFaultBlockerComponent` (`Critical => true`, `DefaultMode => Enabled`) ticks every frame without an `IsLive` guard, its `OnApplicationTick` rethrowing a stored `_terminalFailure` once `DedicatedServer.Core` is in the AppDomain without qualified wrapper guards registered.
- [Verified] A successful teardown calls `JoinRefusal.MarkRestartRequired()`, logging why the process cannot be reused: `Coop's IGameInterface.UnpatchAll is a no-op in this build, so its Harmony patches are still attached to live game methods — inert only while no container exists`. From that point both path 1 and path 2 refuse unconditionally until the game is restarted.

## The component catalogue

`ICompatComponent` (`TAOM.CoopCompat.Core/ICompatComponent.cs`) is five members wide: `Name`, `DefaultMode`, `Critical`, `Install(Harmony, ComponentMode)`, `OnGameFrameworkReady(ComponentMode)`. `Name` is the literal key an operator types into the `components` block of `coop-taom.json`. `ComponentMode` declares exactly three values, in this order: `Off`, `Observe`, `Enabled`. [Verified]

**83 `ICompatComponent` implementations compile into the assembly; 78 are registered.** `CoopCompatSubModule.OnSubModuleLoad` holds one straight-line block of 78 unconditional `_registry.Add(...)` calls at lines 186-263 (78 lines, 78 calls, nothing interleaved), and `ComponentRegistry` does no reflective discovery: grepping `ComponentRegistry.cs` + `CoopCompatSubModule.cs` for `GetTypes`, `Activator.CreateInstance`, `IsAssignableFrom`, `GetExportedTypes` returns zero hits. The one reflection use is diagnostic: `SafeName` falls back to `component.GetType().Name` (`ComponentRegistry.cs:206`). **Of the 78: 22 are `Critical`, 6 default to `Observe`, the other 72 default to `Enabled`, none defaults to `Off`.** Flag column below: **C** = `Critical` (always default `Enabled`), **O** = defaults to `Observe`; behaviour cells paraphrase each component's own `CompatLog.Info` install message, one step short of what the IL does. [Verified; behaviour cells verified as install-message provenance]

| key | flag | behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `basic-character-skill-load-guard` | C | Unresolved load-time character skill rows return 0 instead of throwing |
| `campaign-world-coalescer-window` | C | Routine campaign flushes bounded to one per 100ms; save/load and ordering flushes stay immediate |
| `career-authority` | C | Missing-only career bootstrap, targeted snapshots, server-request mutations, paused-safe recovery |
| `catch-up-drain-guard` | C | Vendor resume candidate 5000 now also needs every live queue <=256 held for a sustained window |
| `coop-object-manager-null-guard` | C | Null existing objects skip the id-counter update, fall through to `AddExisting` rejection |
| `deterministic-battle-scene` | C | Vendor candidate filtering unchanged; the single random final pick uses server `RandomTerrainSeed` |
| `elite-emissary-protocol` | C | Replaces TAOM's purchase boundary with an authenticated server request/result v2, host and guests |
| `facegen` | C | Installs or accepts the 15-race FaceGen provider, verifying race order |
| `field-camp-coop-suppression` | C | Hides TAOM 2.0.26's Make Camp UI and blocks its local callback in coop; single-player unchanged |
| `legacy-module-conflict` | C | Detects retired `DedicatedServer.*` submodules loaded alongside this module |
| `monster-registration` | C | Registers TAOM creature Monsters with Coop's `IObjectManager` after campaign content loads; pins 73 authored TAOM 2.0.20 Monster identities |
| `raid-loot-isolation` | C | Joined clients read-only during raids; server rewards authoritative; transient raid rosters unmanaged |
| `recruitment-volunteer-null-guard` | C | Contains the dedicated-server NRE in Bannerlord 1.4.8's volunteer refresh |
| `server-boot-fault-blocker` | C | Verifies the qualified wrapper handshake (external pre-save and pre-serving gates) |
| `server-gameplay-settings` | C | Binds server-owned TAOM simulation getters; clients consume the authoritative snapshot |
| `server-log-sink` | C | Early log sink at the wrapper resolver-ready boundary; overrides `Missions.Battles.PuppetDeathApplier` to Warning |
| `starting-gold-authority` | C | Manual-only TransferSave pass-through: no automatic peer lookup, ledger write, or gold mutation |
| `transferred-save-troop-roster-migration` | C | Maps retired TAOM 2.0.23 troop ids through `RetiredTaomTroopMigrationContract.CharacterAliases` |
| `transferred-save-troop-training-guard` | C | Unregistered transferred-save characters contribute zero daily training; registered troops unchanged |
| `transferred-save-troop-wage-guard` | C | Canonicalizes unregistered transferred-save troops in a calculation-only roster at Coop's wage bridge |
| `transferred-save-troop-xp-guard` | C | Retains the prior XP write for unregistered transferred-save characters at the `PartyBase` callback |
| `troop-weight-policy` | C | Forces TAOM's troop-weight feature off on all roles: raw roster bodies, no cap penalty or upgrade shed |
| `battle-frame-hitch-probe` | O | 10-second battle windows record max frame time and >=50/100/250/1000ms hitch counts |
| `ghost-reference-sweep` | O | Sweeps dangling party references every 60s on the server so the next save drops the orphans |
| `join-baseline-repair` | O | Registers live parties missing from the coop object manager before each baseline apply |
| `kingdom-decision-probe` | O | Observes add/remove/vote/resolve plus per-kingdom roster hashes |
| `settings-fingerprint` | O | Shape-verifies the Write/WriteAcross/ComputeAcross seam; invocation still pending TAOM wiring |
| `siege-defense-telemetry` | O | Read-only paired siege-start and assault snapshots |

**The other 50 registered keys** (all non-critical, all default `Enabled`; their behaviour cells are omitted here, full set in `docs/raw/taom-coopcompat/extracted-evidence.json`): `active-mission-map-event-guard`, `agent-mount-state-probe`, `authoritative-party-position`, `barber-style-filter-probe`, `battle-order-timing-probe`, `battle-receiver-spike-hysteresis`, `call-to-war-outcome-authority`, `caravan-initargs-null-guard`, `chat-ribbon-placement`, `compressed-hero-join-transfer`, `control-mode-view-removal-guard`, `donate-prisoner-garrison-guard`, `edoras-player-path-repair`, `encounter-attack-condition-guard`, `encounter-map-event-recovery`, `equipment-diagnostic-coalescer`, `exspouses-guard`, `formation-simulation-cache-guard`, `general-navigation-recovery`, `hero-home-settlement-diagnostic-coalescer`, `invalid-path-target-guard`, `item-grant-command`, `join-baseline-reconciliation`, `kingdom-decision-hero-clan-repair`, `mission-after-start-failure-probe`, `nested-kingdom-decision-deferral`, `null-map-event-encounter-end-guard`, `post-battle-speed-repair`, `puppet-initial-wield`, `raid-post-battle-continuation`, `rivendell-ai-path-probe`, `server-warning-dump-suppression`, `settlement-entry-distance-repair`, `settlement-entry-retry-throttle`, `settlement-leave-menu-logger`, `settlement-leave-routing`, `settlement-walk-around-context-repair`, `siege-deployment-selection-guard`, `siege-destructible-state-guard`, `siege-formation-snapshot`, `special-resource-authority`, `stale-mission-relay-guard`, `stale-settlement-menu-repair`, `tournament-agent-removal-guard`, `tournament-bracket`, `troop-grant-command`, `voice-bank-cache`, `wanderer-introduction-null-template-guard`, `wotr-full-war-reconciliation`, `wotr-momentum-relay`. [Verified]

**Tunables inside those omitted rows:** `authoritative-party-position` sends targeted position checkpoints every 1s, clients correct at 0.5+ map units; `agent-mount-state-probe` samples every 1s, capped at 60 transitions; `battle-order-timing-probe` 160 records per mission, read-only; `chat-ribbon-placement` pins MarginBottom 280 in missions (Coop's authored value elsewhere); `general-navigation-recovery` scans 128 parties per 0.25s, 20s stall, 10s repath grace, server only; `rivendell-ai-path-probe` samples every 5s within 30 map units of `town_R1`; `settlement-leave-menu-logger` records the first 40; `siege-formation-snapshot` one line every 20s, max 24; `equipment-diagnostic-coalescer` and `hero-home-settlement-diagnostic-coalescer` keep the first and every 1000th (`AutoSync.Equipment_DynamicPatches._equipmentType_Intercept`, and Coop v0.1.4's client error sink); `caravan-initargs-null-guard` targets the exact Coop v0.1.3 handler. [Verified]

**Setting any `Critical` component to `observe` or `off` faults the whole module, not just that component.** `ComponentRegistry.InstallAll` resolves the mode through `_config.ModeFor`, then checks `Critical` before the `Off` short-circuit (`ComponentRegistry.cs:73-84`), logging `critical component requested as {mode}; session faulted` and calling `CompatState.MarkFaulted`. Once faulted, every remaining component is skipped with `NOT installed: the module already faulted earlier in this pass` and the pass ends in `RollBackAll("module faulted during install")`, unpatching and deactivating everything already live; the `OnGameFrameworkReady` pass aborts the same way, reason `module faulted during the framework-ready pass`. A critical component that throws inside `Install` faults identically through the `catch` at `ComponentRegistry.cs:99-102`. `InstallAll` also faults up front if two registered components share a name case-insensitively (`duplicate component names registered: ...`); the 78 extracted keys are all distinct, so it never fires in this build. [Verified]

**A single config typo disables the module wholesale.** A key matching no registered component is an error, not a warning (`coop-taom.json: component "X" matches no registered component (typo'd kill switch?)`), as is an unknown top-level key, a missing or non-integer or non-1 `schemaVersion`, a `protocolVersion` that parses but is not 1, a non-integer `protocolVersion`, a `components` value that is not an object, a non-string mode value, two component keys differing only by case, and an unparsable mode string. Any of those makes `CompatConfig.IsValid` false, `ModeFor` then returns `ComponentMode.Off` for every component, and the registry marks `configuration invalid — all components off` before installing anything. Two carve-outs: `ParseJson` returns immediately when `coop-taom.json` does not exist, so an absent file is not an error and every component takes its `DefaultMode`; and a key arriving from a legacy `*-disabled.flag` file gets only a warning, `legacy flag "X" matches no registered component — ignored`, and is dropped from the mode map (`CompatConfig.ValidateComponents`, lines 95-106). Flag files are scanned by `Directory.GetFiles(dir, "*-disabled.flag")` in two directories, `CompatConfig.ModuleRoot()` and the assembly's own bin directory; `coopserverlog-disabled.flag` aliases to `server-log-sink`. [Verified]

**27 registered components refuse `Observe` with an explicit `throw` inside `Install`**, all under the same `if (mode != ComponentMode.Enabled)` guard (30 of the 83 implementations carry it, 3 of those unregistered). Verbatim samples: `"coop-object-manager-null-guard has no observe mode"`, `"join-baseline-reconciliation is a mutation and cannot run in observe mode"`, `"the 15-race provider has no safe observe mode"`, `"raid loot isolation cannot run partially or in observe mode"`. Twenty of the 27 are `Critical`, so the fault path catches them first and the throw never runs. The other **seven are not critical**: `caravan-initargs-null-guard`, `equipment-diagnostic-coalescer`, `hero-home-settlement-diagnostic-coalescer`, `join-baseline-reconciliation`, `kingdom-decision-hero-clan-repair`, `siege-destructible-state-guard`, `wanderer-introduction-null-template-guard`. Set one of those to `observe` and the registry catches the exception, logs `install FAILED — rolling back and disabling this component`, unpatches it and carries on: the session survives with that guard silently absent. Use `off` to disable them honestly. Exactly two `Critical` components have no `Install`-time non-`Enabled` throw, and both are unreachable in a non-`Enabled` mode because the registry faults first: `monster-registration` throws only on `Off` (`MonsterRegistrationComponent.cs:65`, `"The component registry must not install an off component."`) and otherwise stores the mode for the framework-ready lifecycle check; `legacy-module-conflict` has no top-of-`Install` mode guard at all, branching on `mode == ComponentMode.Observe` twice (`LegacyModuleConflictComponent.cs:44` and `:61`) to downgrade both the enumeration failure and the retired-module-active refusal to `CompatLog.Warn`. [Verified]

**Five keys do not exist.** They implement `ICompatComponent` and compile in, but nothing constructs them (grep for `new <Class>` and for the bare class name across both decompiled trees hits nothing outside each class's own file), so typing one into `coop-taom.json` is a hard config error, not a switch: `army-registration-repair`, `battle-size-ceiling`, `battle-spawn-reliability` (these three declare `Critical => true`, dead weight in this build), `battle-uplink-guard`, `location-npc-equipment-sampling-guard`. All five would default to `Enabled`. `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent` is `internal sealed`, the only one of the 83 that is not `public sealed`, so a naive `public.*class.*ICompatComponent` grep misses it. [Verified]

**Which assemblies they reach into**, classifying each registered component by evidence in its own `.cs` file (a `using` that is not `TAOM.CoopCompat.*`, or a quoted type-name literal):

| bucket | count | criterion |
|---|---|---|
| Reaches TAOM's own assemblies | **8** | `using TAOM.Features.*` / `TAOM.Adapters`, or a `"TAOM.Features…"` / `"TAOM.Adapters…"` / `"TAOM.IoC"` literal |
| Binds Coop-side assemblies | **46** | `using` or quoted literal naming `Coop.*`, `GameInterface`, `Missions`, `DedicatedServer`, or the generated `AutoSync` patches |
| Neither, so `TaleWorlds` / `SandBox` targets only | **26** | no match on either set |

The TAOM eight: `career-authority`, `elite-emissary-protocol`, `field-camp-coop-suppression`, `server-gameplay-settings`, `settings-fingerprint`, `siege-defense-telemetry`, `special-resource-authority`, `troop-weight-policy`. Two more reach TAOM only through a companion file, so a file-local grep misses them: `wotr-full-war-reconciliation` resolves `"TAOM.IoC"` and `"TAOM.Features.Diplomacy.IWarOfTheRingService"` in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/FullWarHostileReconciliationBehavior.cs` (lines 16, 18, 79, 221), and `wotr-momentum-relay` resolves `"TAOM.IoC"` in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Relay/TaomMomentumSeam.cs` (lines 11, 192), so ten of the 78 touch TAOM. `career-authority` and `elite-emissary-protocol` land in both the TAOM and Coop buckets, which is why 8 + 46 + 26 sums to 80 rather than 78. Also mechanical: **15 of the 78 pin an assembly MVID as a string literal before patching** (`coop-object-manager-null-guard` pins `GameInterface, Version=1.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=null` at MVID `3c2da906-f6ec-443d-afa8-aad1c8286c03`, `CoopObjectManagerNullGuardComponent.cs:18` and `:72`); seven distinct MVIDs appear across the component files, `3c2da906-...` the most cited at 17 occurrences. [Verified as a mechanical classification; which bucket a component "really" belongs in when it resolves its target some other way is [Inference].]

`server-gameplay-settings` is the deepest reach. Its `Keys` map holds **39** fully-qualified TAOM settings-provider member paths (`TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent.cs:19-59`) across the `ArmyTargeting`, `BanditManagement`, `BattleBalance`, `Diplomacy`, `SettlementEconomy`, `SettlementFood` and `WarOfTheRingMomentum` providers. `Install` patches a getter for each, then patches a fortieth, `TAOM.Features.Diplomacy.TaomSettingsProvider.IsAvailable`, resolved by type plus the member name `"IsAvailable"` rather than by a full path literal; the install log reports 39 because its counter only covers the `Keys` loop. `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.DefinitionArray` separately declares 46 server-owned setting definitions, so the patched-getter count and the wire-protocol definition count are different numbers and must not be conflated. [Verified]

## The patch shield, corrected

`CoopPatchShieldComponent` does not disarm TAOM's Harmony patches so Coop can drive the campaign. It does two things: it removes TAOM's own exception-swallowing finalizers, and it forces the process-constant `CoopPresence.IsActive`. That getter is read at eight sites across six files, and only one of them is the install gate.

**Toolchain pin (load-bearing: enum order and priority constants are version-dependent).** [Verified] `ilspycmd 9.1.0.7988` (ICSharpCode.Decompiler 9.1.0.7988) run against `Dependencies/bin/Debug/net472/0Harmony.dll`; `[Reflection.AssemblyName]::GetAssemblyName` reports assembly version **2.4.2.0**.

**Shape: not a component, not in the registry.** [Verified] `public static class CoopPatchShieldComponent` (`main/TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/CoopPatchShieldComponent.cs:8`), so it cannot implement `ICompatComponent`, whose five instance members are `Name`, `DefaultMode`, `Critical`, `Install(Harmony, ComponentMode)`, `OnGameFrameworkReady(ComponentMode)` (`main/TAOM.CoopCompat.Core/ICompatComponent.cs:7-15`). The decompiled tree holds 318 `.cs` files (313 under `main/`, 5 under `boot/`); grepping the type name returns the declaration plus three call sites, all in `CoopCompatSubModule.cs`: `TryInstallEarly()` at `:171` (ctor, declared `:169`) and `:180` (`OnSubModuleLoad`, declared `:174`), `LogStatus()` at `:181`. The `_registry.Add(...)` block is contiguous `:186-:263`, **78 entries**, with `_registry.InstallAll()` at `:264`; no entry is the shield. It sits outside the component lifecycle: no mode, no `Critical` flag, no deactivation path.

**What the double call buys.** [Verified] TAOM installs the shield exactly twice, the only two `PatchShield.Install()` call sites in the repo: `Dependencies/SubModule.cs:234` (pass 1, `OnSubModuleLoad`, declared `:164`) and `:288` (pass 2, `OnGameInitializationFinished(Game game)`, declared `:273`), each preceded by `CoopPresence.Refresh()` (`:225`, `:285`). The comment at `:283-284` says the launcher's active-module list "is reliably populated by now, so this is the read that actually decides the unpatch/swallow policy for the session". [Inference] Which of the compat's two calls does the work depends on module load order, and the vendor treats that as unproven: `KNOWN-ISSUES.txt:13` lists "flag-free PatchShield policy across actual module-load orders" under the `:11-12` heading for areas with static regression coverage but no multiplayer runtime evidence.

[Inference] The ctor call is not the module's earliest managed code. `Payload/Modules/TAOM.CoopCompat/SubModule.xml:25-31` declares `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.BootstrapSubModule` ahead of `CoopCompatSubModule` with the comment "MUST remain first", and that type exists (`boot/TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap/BootstrapSubModule.cs`, 11 lines, ctor at `:7`, no shield or presence reference). On the engine-lifecycle question the nearest artifact is a TAOM code comment (`Main/SubModule.cs:775-781`) recording a Codex reading of decompiled v1.4.7 that `Module.Initialize` populates `ModuleHelper`'s `_loadedModules` before `LoadSubModules`, so "even a SubModule constructor already sees it". That is secondhand, was not re-read against the engine here, and if true it makes the `OnSubModuleLoad` call a retry rather than an ordering hedge.

[Verified] Prefix idempotence is explicit: `PatchOnce` (`CoopPatchShieldPolicy.cs:58-72`) reads `Harmony.GetPatchInfo(target)` and skips when a prefix already exists with owner `taom.coopcompat.coop-patchshield-policy` and the same `PatchMethod` (single-line predicate at `:65`). [Inference] The unpatch sweep is idempotent only as a consequence: after a first pass no method carries the shield owner, so `Owners.Contains` at `:49` fails for every entry on a second pass.

**The status string is not idempotent, and that costs the log its only number.** [Verified] `_status` is overwritten unconditionally on all three paths (`CoopPatchShieldComponent.cs:43`, `:47`, `:51`), and `CompatLog.Open()` runs at `CoopCompatSubModule.cs:179`, before the second `TryInstallEarly` (`:180`) and `LogStatus()` (`:181`); `CompatLog.cs:29` writes `coopcompat-<pid>.log`. When the constructor pass does the removing, the log says `removed=0` and the real count exists only in the constructor's `Console.WriteLine`, emitted before the log file was opened. A ctor success followed by a second-call throw is logged only as the warning.

**The gate.** [Verified] `TryInstallEarly` reflects `TaleWorlds.ModuleManager.ModuleHelper.GetModuleInfo(string)` (`Component:16`), iterates `string[] array = new string[2] { "Coop", "CoopNightly" }` (`:18`), invokes at `:22`, reads `IsActive` off the returned object at `:23`, and hands the pair to `CoopPatchShieldPolicy.IsCoopModule`, exact ordinal equality against those same two ids (`Policy:14-25`, `StringComparison.Ordinal` at `:18` and `:20`). If neither resolves it returns before touching Harmony with `_status = "no selected Coop/CoopNightly module; no changes"` (`Component:41-45`). [Verified] The two sides compare ids with different semantics: TAOM's `CoopPresencePolicy.MatchKnown` builds its known-id set with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase` (`Dependencies/Foundation/CoopPresencePolicy.cs:56`, dedupe at `:62`), so a launcher id differing only in case is a co-op module to TAOM and not to the compat.

**What `Install()` actually does.** [Verified] Harmony instance id `"taom.coopcompat.coop-patchshield-policy"` (`Policy:10` as `const string Owner`, `:41` as the `new Harmony(...)` argument, `:65` in the dedupe predicate); both prefixes carry `priority = 800` (`:69`). 800 is `HarmonyLib.Priority.First`, top of the nine named constants (`Last=0, VeryLow=100, Low=200, LowerThanNormal=300, Normal=400, HigherThanNormal=500, High=600, VeryHigh=700, First=800`), but not a maximum: `Priority` is a static class of `const int`, not an enum, and `HarmonyMethod.priority` is a plain `public int priority = -1`, so nothing enforces a ceiling.

| Step | Target | Patch | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| a | `CoopPresence.IsActive` getter via `GetProperty("IsActive", Static\|Public)?.GetGetMethod()` (`:35`) | prefix `ReportCoopActive(ref bool __result)` (`:74-78`) | `__result = true; return false;` Forced true for every caller, original probe skipped. |
| b | `PatchShield.Install()` via `GetMethod("Install", Static\|Public, null, Type.EmptyTypes, null)` (`:36`) | prefix `SkipShieldInstall()` (`:80-83`) | `return false;` The method body never runs. |
| c | every entry of `Harmony.GetAllPatchedMethods()` (loop `:45-54`) | `Unpatch(m, (HarmonyPatchType)0, "TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield")` (`:51`) | Removes patches owned by that one literal string. Count returned. |

[Verified] `(HarmonyPatchType)0` is `All` (declaration order `All, Prefix, Postfix, Transpiler, Finalizer, ReversePatch, InnerPrefix, InnerPostfix`). The selector `patchInfo.Owners.Contains("TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield")` (`:49`) runs on `Patches.Owners`, declared `public ReadOnlyCollection<string>`, **not** a `HashSet<string>`: its body `UnionWith`s the six patch collections (`Prefixes`, `Postfixes`, `Transpilers`, `Finalizers`, `InnerPrefixes`, `InnerPostfixes`) into a local set used only for de-duplication, then returns `hashSet.ToList().AsReadOnly()`. So `Contains` goes through `EqualityComparer<string>.Default`: case-sensitive exact equality, never a prefix. Removal is surgical rather than a revert: `PatchProcessor.Unpatch(HarmonyPatchType, string)` (`:142-183`) forwards to `RemovePrefix` / `RemovePostfix` / `RemoveTranspiler` / `RemoveFinalizer` / `RemoveInnerPrefix` / `RemoveInnerPostfix`, each reaching `PatchInfo.Remove(owner, current)` and `current.Where(patch => patch.owner != owner)` (the `"*"` wildcard branch is not taken), then `PatchFunctions.UpdateWrapper(original, patchInfo)` at `:179` rebuilds the detour around every surviving owner.

**Step (b), not step (a), is what suppresses the install.** [Verified] `PatchShield.Install()` reads `CoopPresence.IsActive` at `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:125` and evaluates `ShouldInstall` at `:126`, both inside the body `SkipShieldInstall` skips, so (a) contributes nothing to the install decision. Success status (`Component:47`): `"selected=" + text + "; co-op presence enforced; PatchShield install suppressed; existing shielded methods removed=" + num2 + "; no flag files required"`.

| `CoopPresence.IsActive` read site | What forcing `true` changes |
|---|---|
| `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:125` | The install gate. Unreachable once (b) is applied. |
| `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs:326` | `ShouldUnpatchForeignOwners(true)` is false (`PatchShieldPolicy.cs:110`), so an already-installed shield records the target in `_withheld` (`:332`) and logs "would unpatch ... (withheld)" (`:370`) instead of stripping a foreign owner. |
| `Dependencies/Foundation/SaveShield.cs:273`, `:286` | `SaveShieldPolicy.ShouldSwallow(category, true, false)` returns false for `SAVE-LOAD` (`SaveShieldPolicy.cs:30-36`), so save-load faults are **rethrown**. `MISSION-INIT` keeps swallowing. |
| `Main/Features/CoopInterop/CoopPresenceProvider.cs:9` | `ICoopPresenceProvider.IsCoopActive` reads true mod-wide. |
| `Main/Features/CoopInterop/CoopSessionProvider.cs:87`, `:103` | Skips the "no module, therefore solo" short circuit and runs the reflection role probe feeding `ShouldDeferToHost`. |
| `Main/SubModule.cs:782` | UI registration takes the co-op-filtered branch (`:804-815`) instead of unfiltered `extender.Register(assembly)` (`:793-801`). |

[Verified] Step (a) patches only the getter (`Policy:35`); `ActiveCoopModuleIds` and `ExtraProtectedOwnerPrefixes` (`CoopPresence.cs:98-107`) are unpatched and still run `EnsureProbed()`, so presence goes internally inconsistent: `IsActive` true while the id list reports whatever the launcher actually had. [Verified] `Main/SubModule.cs:1640-1641` gates the `[HarmonyCensus]` writer on `ActiveCoopModuleIds.Count > 0`, so the census does not fire on forced presence alone.

**The correction: there is no list of TAOM methods.** [Verified] Across `main/` and `boot/`: exactly one `.Unpatch(` call site (`Policy:51`), one `Harmony.GetAllPatchedMethods()` call (`Policy:45`), two files whose name contains `Shield` (the two under discussion), and four `"TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.*"` string-literal hits carrying two distinct type names, `CoopPresence` and `PatchShield` (`Component:46`; `Policy:12`, `:49`, `:51`). [Verified] The long `"TAOM.Features.*"` tables elsewhere in the tree are not one component's and none is a shield target list: 93 lines across 19 files, `TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent.cs` holding 40 (settings keys), `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs` 13, `TaomEmissarySeam.cs` 7, then `TaomMomentumSeam.cs`, `FullWarHostileReconciliationBehavior.cs` and `EmissaryPurchaseServerHandler.cs` at 4 each, and 13 further files at 1 to 3.

**What gets removed, and the bound on it.** [Verified] `PatchShield.cs` is 429 lines and contains exactly one `.Patch(` call (`:215-216`, finalizer-only), running under `private const string HarmonyId = "TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield"` (`:36`) on the instance created at `:142`. `TryUnpatchOffendingPatches` (declared `:301`, called from `:291`) is not a second write site: it builds its own `new Harmony(HarmonyId)` at `:355` and only calls `Unpatch` (`:376-378`), which registers no owner. So `All` for that owner resolves to exactly the shield finalizers. [Verified] The removable set is narrower than "everything Harmony had patched" because `Install` never shielded three classes: any method whose declaring assembly name starts with `TAOM` (`:177-183`, `OrdinalIgnoreCase`), anything under `TaleWorlds.GauntletUI` / `TaleWorlds.TwoDimension` / `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade.GauntletUI` (`:60-69`, checked at `:190-195`), and any method `SaveShield.IsShielding` already covers (`:203-208`).

**Registrations survive; fault behaviour does not.** [Verified] Exact-equality matching spares `com.taom.mod` (the sole `new Harmony(...)` in `Main/`, `Main/SubModule.cs:162`) plus four other TAOM-owned ids: `TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.SaveShield` (`SaveShield.cs:43`), `...CollectAssemblyTypesShim` (`CollectAssemblyTypesShim.cs:27`), `...SubModuleConstructionGuard` (`SubModuleConstructionGuard.cs:37`), `TAOM.HarmonyGuard` (`Dependencies/SubModule.cs:19`, as `GuardHarmonyId`). [Verified] But because `Install` skips only methods whose *declaring* assembly starts with `TAOM` (`:177-183`), every `com.taom.mod` patch on a `TaleWorlds.*` target was shielded; removing the finalizer means a `MissingMethodException`, `MissingFieldException` or `TypeLoadException` raised inside one of those patches now propagates instead of being swallowed and counted (`PatchShield.ShouldSwallow`, `:260-299`). That is the intended trade under co-op and it is a behaviour change: "every TAOM patch survives untouched" is true of registration only.

**It mirrors a decision TAOM already made.** [Verified] `PatchShieldPolicy.cs:151-152` is `public static bool ShouldInstall(bool coopActive, bool disabledByFlag) => !disabledByFlag && !coopActive;`, restated character for character at `docs/features/coop-interop.md:431` inside §"PatchShield is skipped under co-op" (heading `:400`, section `:400-436`) and framed there as a performance fix: a shield finalizer binds `__originalMethod`, so Harmony's wrapper pays a `MethodBase.GetMethodFromHandle` plus a try/catch on every call, about 50 microseconds (`:406`), the mechanism behind the measured 104 to 109 second freeze in #331 (`:407`). [Verified] The **43 campaign types** figure is TAOM's own written assertion about Coop's AutoSync surface (`coop-interop.md:408`, `PatchShieldPolicy.cs:120`, `PatchShield.cs:130`), not independently counted here, so do not present it as measured. [Verified] Doc `:409-410` and the `PatchShieldPolicy` comment `:121-123` give the timing that makes the tax land: Coop's `PatchAll` runs on connect, before `OnGameInitializationFinished`, so it is **pass 2** that shields that surface. [Verified] The doc says outright (`:428-435`) that this makes **two** community-recipe steps redundant under co-op, `patchshield-disabled.flag` and `saveshield-swallow-disabled.flag`, the latter still doing strictly more because it also rethrows the `MISSION-INIT` chain co-op deliberately leaves swallowing.

**Whether TAOM's own probe would have agreed.** [Verified] `CoopPresence` fails closed: `ReadActiveModuleIds` returns an empty list on any exception (`CoopPresence.cs:157-168`, `:166`), `LogProbeResult` logs "active module list unavailable; treating co-op as NOT present" (`:175`), and `IsActive` is `_activeCoopModuleIds.Count > 0` (`:94`). Its compiled defaults are exactly three ids, `BannerlordTogether`, `BattleLinkMPClient`, `Coop` (`:53-63`), and the shipped `Dependencies/_Module/coop-modules.txt` `[modules]` block lists the same three (`:14`, `:15`, `:18`) with no `CoopNightly`. [Verified] The supported build depends on `<DependedModule Id="CoopNightly" />` (`Payload/Modules/TAOM.CoopCompat/SubModule.xml:14`, with `<DependedModuleMetadata id="CoopNightly" order="LoadBeforeThis" version="v0.1.4" />` at `:19`), and `PACKAGE.template.json:36-39` pins `"launcherOrder": ["CoopNightly", "TAOM.CoopCompat"]` under `"launcherPolicy": "exact-supported-tuple"` (`:40`). [Inference] Bannerlord resolves `DependedModule` ids against a module's own `<Id>`, so the supported co-op module registers `CoopNightly`, which is absent from TAOM's compiled list.

[Verified] That gap is closed twice on a supported install, without step (a). `RuntimeLog.ModuleDir` resolves `Modules/TAOM.Dependencies/` by walking back three directories from the loaded DLL (`RuntimeLog.cs:34-56`); `Tools/Install-Client.ps1:57` declares `$script:TaomPolicyFlagNames = @('coop-force-active.flag', 'patchshield-disabled.flag')`, `:965-974` targets each at `<GameRoot>\Modules\TAOM.Dependencies\<flag>` (`:968`), and `:1122-1127` writes each as a zero-byte file with `[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes(..., [byte[]]@())`, throwing "TAOM co-op policy flag was not created" if it is absent afterward. [Verified] `coop-force-active.flag` alone is sufficient with no managed compat code: it is `CoopPresence.ForceActiveFlagName` (`:47`), read by `IsForcedActive()` (`:198-207`) and passed into `CoopPresencePolicy.ResolveActiveIds` (`:146`, `:150-151`), which returns `{ "(forced-by-flag)" }` when detection found nothing and the flag is present (`CoopPresencePolicy.cs:44-45`, marker const `:22`); `IsActive` is a count test, so the flag makes it true. [Verified] Separately `:1121` calls `Set-TaomCoopModuleIdConfigured` (function keyword `:822`, `param([string]$Path, [string]$ModuleId = 'CoopNightly')` on `:823`), inserting `CoopNightly` under the `[modules]` header of `<GameRoot>\Modules\TAOM.Dependencies\coop-modules.txt` (`:964`) so detection itself succeeds; `PACKAGE.template.json:46-49` records both flags under `supportedBaseline.taomCoopPolicyFlags` and `:61-64` records `"taomCoopModuleIds": ["Coop", "CoopNightly"]`. So the C# path is a third layer behind two file-level ones, and "no flag files required" describes a capability, not the shipped configuration; on a supported install step (a) duplicates a file the installer just wrote. [Verified] `PACKAGE.json:159` records the residual risk: "v0.3.15 enforces co-op presence and suppresses PatchShield without flags; exact client/server CLR hooks pass but live flag-free startup after a TAOM update remains pending".

**One identifier does not reconcile across the artifacts.** [Verified] `PACKAGE.template.json:65` pins `"coopIdentity": "workshop-3770450698-v0.1.4-20260830"`, while `docs/features/coop-interop.md:45-46` records that same Workshop item, 3770450698, as registering launcher id **`Coop`**, repeated in `Dependencies/_Module/coop-modules.txt:16-18` and in `CoopPresence.cs:57-62` (a comment citing `<Id value="Coop"/>` from a 2026-08-01 decompile). One Workshop id, two launcher ids, artifacts about a month apart: either the module renamed its `<Id>` or one artifact is stale. The compat's own `SubModule.xml` is the stronger signal for the shipped tuple, and either reading leaves TAOM's compiled list without `CoopNightly`, so the practical consequence is unchanged.

**The finding: four strings, and a rename fails silently.** [Verified] Component and policy hardcode **eleven** distinct string literals: `"TaleWorlds.ModuleManager.ModuleHelper"` and `"GetModuleInfo"` (`Component:16`), `"Coop"` and `"CoopNightly"` (`Component:18`, `Policy:18`/`:20`), `"IsActive"`, `"Install"`, the two `TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.*` type names, the Harmony owner id, and the two prefix method names resolved by string at `Policy:42-43`. Four reach into TAOM, with no fallback and no version check: type names `"TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.CoopPresence"` and `"TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield"` (both `Component:46`), the same `PatchShield` string again as Harmony owner id (`Policy:12`, `:49`, `:51`), property `"IsActive"` (`Policy:35`, also `Component:23` for the launcher probe), and method `"Install"` (`Policy:36`).

[Verified] The `PatchShield` literal carries two independent roles and only one is guarded. As a **type name** it feeds `AccessTools.TypeByName`, so a rename nulls the chain through the null-conditionals and hits the guard at `Policy:37-40`, which throws `MissingMethodException("TAOM co-op presence/PatchShield API changed")` when `methodInfo?.ReturnType != typeof(bool) || methodInfo2?.ReturnType != typeof(void)`. As a **Harmony owner id** it is compared against `PatchShield.cs:36`'s separate `HarmonyId` const, whose value merely happens to equal the type's full name: change that const alone and nothing throws, the guard is satisfied, (a) and (b) apply, and (c) matches no owner, reporting `removed=0` while every shield finalizer stays attached.

[Verified] The guard sits above `new Harmony(...)` at `:41`, so **the API-shape failure specifically** is all-or-nothing; that is not a general guarantee against a half-patched state. `PatchOnce` runs twice (`:42`, `:43`) and the sweep at `:45-54` has neither a per-`Unpatch` try/catch nor a guard on `Harmony.GetAllPatchedMethods()`, exactly the pair TAOM's own equivalent wraps (`PatchShield.cs:156-164` around the enumeration, `:375-390` around each `Unpatch`). A throw there leaves (a) applied with (b) and (c) skipped or partial, reported with the same warning string as a clean bail. [Inference] The (a)-only half-state is benign for the install decision, since (a) alone drives `ShouldInstall` to false. [Verified] The exception dies in `TryInstallEarly`'s catch-all (`Component:49-52`), which sets `_status = "WARNING: early co-op policy unavailable: " + ex.GetType().Name + ": " + ex.Message`, and `Console.WriteLine("[TAOM.CoopCompat] " + _status)` at `:53` sits outside the try so it runs on every path. Nothing rethrows, nothing refuses the join; `LogStatus()` (`:56-59`) writes the same string through `CompatLog.Info` prefixed `"coop-patchshield-policy | "`, and `OnSubModuleLoad` continues into `CompatLog.Info("boot | ...")` (`CoopCompatSubModule.cs:182`) and the registry build (`:185`). The module boots normally.

[Inference] The resulting failure mode needs three conditions at once, none of which hold on a supported install: `patchshield-disabled.flag` absent (else `PatchShield.IsDisabled()` at `:106-115` makes `disabledByFlag` true and `ShouldInstall` false regardless of co-op), `coop-force-active.flag` absent, and the launcher id absent from both `CompiledModuleDefaults` and `coop-modules.txt`. Where all three hold, `CoopPresence.IsActive` reads false and three things follow, all traced from source rather than measured: (1) `ShouldInstall(coopActive: false, disabledByFlag: false)` returns true, both passes install, and pass 2 wraps Coop's AutoSync surface in per-call finalizers, the frame-rate collapse `ShouldInstall`'s doc comment describes (`:115-150`), not a crash; (2) `ShouldUnpatchForeignOwners(false)` returns true (`PatchShieldPolicy.cs:110`), re-arming the mid-session rescue strip at `PatchShield.cs:326-327`, and Coop's four owner ids are compiled into `CompiledProtectedOwnerPrefixes` (`PatchShieldPolicy.cs:59-62`) so Coop is spared while a third mod's sync-relevant patches are not; (3) `SaveShieldPolicy.ShouldSwallow(category, false, false)` returns true for every category including `SAVE-LOAD` (`SaveShieldPolicy.cs:32-35`), restoring the swallow co-op deliberately turns off. TAOM's own doc comments call outcomes 2 and 3 save-corrupting, so "silent performance regression" understates it. The only signal is one line: `[TAOM.CoopCompat] WARNING: early co-op policy unavailable: MissingMethodException: TAOM co-op presence/PatchShield API changed`.

[Verified] The build's own manifest already grades this exact surface unproven at `PACKAGE.json:112`, `"patchShieldRuntimeProven": false`, under `liveAcceptance` (status at `:111`, `"v0.3.15-coop-v0.1.4-migration-runtime-unproven"`). Do not cite the generic `"runtimeProven": false` at `:108` for this: it sits under `diagnostics`, alongside `sessionLogMaxBytes`, `sessionLogRetentionCount` and `collectors`; `PACKAGE.template.json:88` is the same diagnostics field, and the template carries no `patchShieldRuntimeProven` key at all.

**Weigh the kit's own numbers accordingly.** [Verified] The package disagrees with itself by one on a protocol constant: `WHAT-CHANGED.txt:5` and `LIVE-TEST-SUMMARY.txt:5` both state "TAOM settings protocol is now 3" and `PACKAGE.json:156` repeats "0.3.15 changes TAOM settings protocol to 3", while the machine-readable `protocols.taomServerSettings` field records **2** in both `PACKAGE.json:22` and `PACKAGE.template.json:19`. Resolving that is out of scope; it is the reason to read the manifest fields rather than the shipped prose when a number matters.

**Side observation, out of scope, worth someone's attention.** [Verified] `PatchShieldPolicy.CompiledProtectedOwnerPrefixes` protects the prefix `"TAOM"` (`:23`, `:25`) and `IsProtectedOwner` matches with `StartsWith(prefix, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)` (`:84-95`, test at `:92`); `com.taom.mod` does not start with `TAOM` under that test. Whether TAOM's own rescue path could therefore strip `com.taom.mod` is a separate question, and `ShouldUnpatchForeignOwners` (`:110`) already disables the strip whenever co-op is active. That belongs in `/investigate` or `/deep-review` on `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShieldPolicy.cs`, not here.

## The wire protocols

- **It rides Coop's message bus, not LiteNetLib.** [Verified] 26 protobuf-net messages (`Network*.cs`), all in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Relay`, all `[ProtoContract(SkipConstructor = true)]`, all numbering fields `[ProtoMember(n)]` with `[ProtoMember(1)]` reserved for `int ProtocolVersion`. 22 are `sealed class : ICommand, IMessage`; the 4 battle-spawn types (`NetworkBattleSpawnReceiverReady`, `NetworkBattleSpawnReplayAnnounce`, `NetworkBattleSpawnReplayAttemptEnded`, `NetworkBattleSpawnTransferAck`) are `readonly struct : IEvent, IMessage`. `ICommand`, `IEvent` and `IMessage` come from `Common.Messaging`.
- **Two break the shape.** [Verified] `NetworkCompressedNewPlayerHeroCreatedV1` declares `public int ProtocolVersion { get; private set; }`; the other 25 use `public readonly int ProtocolVersion`. [Verified] `NetworkTaomBattleSizePolicyV1` alone does not hardcode its version (`ProtocolVersion = policy.ProtocolVersion`, off the `BattleSizeAuthoritySnapshot` it is built from), but `BattleSizeAuthorityPolicy.TryValidate` returns false with `"unsupported protocol version"` for anything but 1, so a validated snapshot can only carry 1.
- **Handlers are declared into Coop's namespaces.** [Verified] 10 handler types plus `CareerClientEndpoint` (`internal static class`) into `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat`, 6 into `Coop.Core.Server.Services.TaomCoopCompat`. All 16 are `public sealed class X : IHandler, IDisposable`, with 33 `_broker.Subscribe<T>(...)` against 33 `Unsubscribe<T>`, balanced per file. [Verified] The decompiled `RunSafe` file arguments carry 12 distinct build paths under `C:\Users\<author>\Documents\Bannerlord Coop\src\TAOM.CoopCompat\Relay\`; only 3 are feature files (`BattleSizeAuthorityHandlers.cs`, `FullWarDiplomacyHandlers.cs`, `TaomServerSettingsHandlers.cs`), the other 9 carry one-type names.
- **Constructors are not uniform.** [Verified] Only 9 of 16 take exactly `(IMessageBroker, INetwork)` (client BattleSize, EmissaryPurchase, FullWarDiplomacy, SpecialResourceAuthority, TaomServerSettings, WotrRelay; server BattleSize, FullWarDiplomacy, TaomServerSettings), and 3 of those 9 accept it and never store it (`SpecialResourceAuthorityClientHandler`, `EmissaryPurchaseClientHandler`, `WotrRelayClientHandler`), all pure receivers: only 11 of 16 declare an `INetwork` or `IBattleNetwork` field. The 7 deviating constructors:
  - `AuthoritativePositionClientHandler` `(IMessageBroker, IObjectManager)`, no `INetwork` at all: receive-only, `_objects` resolves the party
  - `CompressedHeroJoinTransferClientHandler` `(IMessageBroker, ICommonSerializer, IMessagePacketHandler)`
  - `BattleSpawnReliabilityClientHandler` `(IMessageBroker, IBattleNetwork, IControllerIdProvider, IMissionContext)`
  - `CareerAuthorityClientHandler` `(IMessageBroker, INetwork, IPlayerManager, IObjectManager, IControllerIdProvider)`
  - `CareerAuthorityServerHandler` and `SpecialResourceAuthorityServerHandler` `(IMessageBroker, INetwork, IPlayerManager, IObjectManager)`
  - `EmissaryPurchaseServerHandler` `(IMessageBroker, INetwork, IPlayerManager, IObjectManager, IControllerIdProvider)`
- **Sending.** [Verified] In-handler the injected `_network` is used directly: 7 `_network.Send(peer, (IMessage)(object)msg)` sites plus 6 `_network.SendAll(...)`, 13 across the two handler namespaces. Outside a handler (campaign behaviors, relay endpoints) the transport resolves late through `GameInterface.ContainerProvider.TryResolve<INetwork>(ref val)`, 8 sites, and those 8 do not share one failure policy. [Verified] One family bypasses `INetwork` entirely: `BattleSpawnReliabilityClientHandler` sends through `Missions.Services.Network.IBattleNetwork.Send(controllerId, IMessage)` at 6 sites, addressing a peer by controller id inside a live mission.

| Late-resolve site | On a failed resolve |
|---|---|
| `FullWarDiplomacyEndpoint.cs:119` | The only true cache-and-retry. `PublishAuthoritativeSnapshot` writes `_serverCampaignDay`, `_serverFactionAIds`, `_serverFactionBIds` and `_serverAtWar` under `Sync` **before** resolving, then logs `wotr-full-war-client-sync \| authoritative snapshot cached but Coop INetwork was unavailable; a later client request can retry` once behind `_broadcastFailureLogged` and returns |
| `AuthoritativePartyPositionComponent.cs:88` (1 Hz tick), `WotrMomentumRelayBehavior.cs:74` (hourly tick) | Plain skip, nothing cached. Momentum logs once via `LogSeamFailureOnce("Coop INetwork unavailable")` and leaves `_lastSentFingerprint` untouched; position logs nothing |
| `SpecialResourceAuthorityClientEndpoint.cs:100` and `:134` | Data loss, not retry. The spend-report path is a positive `if`, so a failed resolve drops the measured spend with no log. `RequestSync` runs `Interlocked.Increment(ref _syncAttempts)` **before** the resolve, burning one of `MaxSyncAttempts = 4` with nothing sent (only `ResetSession` clears the counter) |
| `EmissaryPurchaseProtocolComponent.cs:113`, `EmissaryPurchaseClientEndpoint.cs:111`, `CampaignWorldCoalescerComponent.cs:214` | Hard error. The first throws `InvalidOperationException("client network is unavailable")` into a catch running `Cancel(requestId)`, `CompatLog.Error` and `DisplayUnavailable(ex.Message)`; the second runs `Cancel(ack.RequestId)` plus `DisplayUnavailable("the client network disappeared after resource sync")`; the coalescer throws `pending roster updates have no live network` into `FaultRuntime`, which calls `CompatState.MarkFaulted("critical component 'campaign-world-coalescer-window' failed at runtime")` and then throws `TAOM.CoopCompat cannot prove safe campaign coalescing; refusing to continue` |

- **Player-visible emissary text.** [Verified] `DisplayUnavailable`'s prefix with the detail appended: `The emissary could not contact the co-op host: <detail>`.
- **Thread hop.** [Verified] Broker callbacks arrive off the game thread, so campaign-touching work is wrapped in `Common.GameThread.RunSafe(action, false, <member>, <file>, <method>)`: 19 sites across 12 of the 16 handler types. Four wrap nothing and reply straight off the callback thread: `BattleSizeAuthorityServerHandler`, `FullWarDiplomacyServerHandler` and `TaomServerSettingsServerHandler` (each reads a locked snapshot and sends), plus `CompressedHeroJoinTransferClientHandler` (calls `_messagePacketHandler.PublishEvent` directly). The rule is "campaign state goes through `RunSafe`", not "every handler does". [Verified] Most `RunSafe` bodies re-read their activation flag after the hop (`if (SpecialResourceActivation.Enabled) { ... }`, same in the emissary and settings handlers), so a fault landing between the broker callback and the game-thread hop still stops the apply.
- **No serializer registration.** [Verified] Grep for `RuntimeTypeModel`, `TypeMapper`, `SerializableTypeMapper`, `AddType(` and `RegisterMessage` across both decompiled trees returns zero hits. Wire identity is entirely Coop's to assign.
- **`legacy-module-conflict`.** [Verified] `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components.LegacyModuleConflictComponent` is `Name => "legacy-module-conflict"`, `Critical => true`, `DefaultMode => ComponentMode.Enabled`, enumerating active modules by reflection on `TaleWorlds.ModuleManager.ModuleHelper.GetActiveModules()` (`AccessTools.TypeByName`, then each element's `Id`). `RetiredModuleIds` (refused, filtered by `Install`): `DedicatedServer.CoopWotrRelay`, `DedicatedServer.WotrRelay`, `DedicatedServer.CoopVoiceCache`, `DedicatedServer.TAOMFaceGen`. `PermittedByOwnerDecision` (warned, allowed, filtered by `Install`): `CoopPartyAttach`. [Verified] `IndependentAndCoLoadable` (`DedicatedServer.CareerDiamonds`) is **dead data with no consumer**: a grep for the field name and for `CareerDiamonds` each returns exactly one hit in the whole decompile, the declaration at `LegacyModuleConflictComponent.cs:17`.
- **The refusal.** [Verified] Hitting the retired list throws `InvalidOperationException`: `module(s) active that cannot be co-loaded with TAOM.CoopCompat [<ids>]`, whose stated reason is that running both double-patches the same seams and puts two incompatible momentum protocols on one broker, because `wire ids are derived from Type.FullName, which changed when the contracts moved`; the remedy is `Untick either the listed module(s) or TAOM.CoopCompat on every machine - the choice is yours and it is a TRADE.` The `WotrRelay` appendix is a substring test (`id.IndexOf("WotrRelay", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) >= 0`), so both retired relay ids trigger it, and it adds that the compat does not yet replace that module's hero-state, career, special-resource or emissary features. [Verified] The `CoopPartyAttach` warning is specific: `PERMITTED BUT UNPROVEN: '<id>' is loaded alongside this module. It was previously refused and was unblocked by owner decision; none of its behaviour is ported here, and it remains the prime suspect for the old stack's mid-fight tournament crashes.` [Verified] It fails **closed**: if `ActiveModuleIds()` throws (missing `ModuleHelper`, `GetActiveModules` or `ModuleInfo.Id`), Enabled mode throws `could not enumerate active modules (...); retired-module conflict could NOT be verified. This is a critical preflight: it refuses rather than assuming no conflict.`
- **The throw does not reach the game as a throw.** [Verified] `ComponentRegistry.InstallAll` catches every `Install` exception, logs `<name> | install FAILED — rolling back and disabling this component`, calls `TryUnpatch` and `TryDeactivate`, and because the component is `Critical` calls `CompatState.MarkFaulted("critical component \"legacy-module-conflict\" failed to install")`. The loop finishes and `RollBackAll("module faulted during install")` unpatches every live component. The player-visible refusal lands later, at the `StartAsClient` / `StartAsServer` prefix and at campaign start.
- **Observe is unreachable for a critical component.** [Verified] `InstallAll` tests `SafeCritical(component) && componentMode != ComponentMode.Enabled` **before** calling `Install`, logs `<name> | critical component requested as {mode}; session faulted`, calls `MarkFaulted($"critical component \"{name}\" must be enabled (requested {mode})")` and `continue`s. So the warn-only strings are dead code: `observe: retired module(s) ACTIVE alongside this one [...] — this would normally refuse to start; expect double patching and a split momentum protocol`, `TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent`'s `server gameplay settings cannot observe`, and `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent`'s `battle-spawn reliability is critical and must be enabled`.
- **The `Type.FullName` derivation itself is not verified here, and "silent" is unproven.** [Inference] `SerializableTypeMapper` appears in neither decompiled tree and no Coop assembly ships in the package: `Payload/` holds exactly 5 files, `SubModule.xml` plus `TAOM.CoopCompat.dll` and `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap.dll` in each of `Win64_Shipping_Client` and `Win64_Shipping_Server`. Coop is pinned by SHA-256 only (`supportedBaseline.coopPins` for `Common.dll`, `Coop.Core.dll`, `Coop.Steam.dll`, `GameInterface.dll`, `Missions.dll`). The only support for the claim is the developer asserting it in that error string; whether a rename surfaces as a deserialization error rather than silence needs `Coop.Core.dll` / `Common.dll` from the CoopNightly 0.1.4 install.
- **Naming has drifted from the versions in 3 of the 26 type names.** [Verified] `NetworkTaomServerSettingsV1` and `NetworkTaomServerSettingsRequestV1` both assign `ProtocolVersion = 3` in their constructors and declare no `CurrentProtocolVersion` constant at all; `NetworkAuthoritativePartyPositionV1` declares `CurrentProtocolVersion = 2` and assigns `2`. Every other type name matches its literal. [Inference] If the type name is the wire id, correcting those to `V3` breaks the wire while changing nothing else.

### The families

[Verified] Every literal below was read from the constant or constructor named in its row, and all ten component `Name` strings were confirmed against the 83 `public string Name =>` declarations in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components`. `Crit` is the component's own `Critical` flag: it decides whether an install failure faults the whole module or only disables that family.

| Family (component `Name`) | Crit | Version constant and literal | Message types | Synchronizes | Authoritative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `authoritative-party-position` | false | `NetworkAuthoritativePartyPositionV1.CurrentProtocolVersion = 2` | `NetworkAuthoritativePartyPositionV1` | Server pushes each connected player's own map position at 1 Hz (`_elapsed < 1f` gate), targeted per `NetPeer` via `Send`, stamped with a server session id, a monotonic `Interlocked.Increment` sequence and `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp()` milliseconds | Server |
| `career-authority` | true | `CareerAuthorityPolicy.ProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkCareerBootstrapClaimV1`, `NetworkCareerSnapshotRequestV1`, `NetworkCareerMutationRequestV1`, `NetworkCareerAuthoritativeSnapshotV1` | Career id, chosen perks, tier unlocks and flags for a player hero, as request plus authoritative snapshot | Server |
| `wotr-full-war-reconciliation` | false | `FullWarDiplomacySnapshotPolicy.ProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkTaomFullWarSnapshotRequestV1`, `NetworkTaomFullWarSnapshotV1` | Hostile-pair graph as three parallel arrays (`FactionAIds`, `FactionBIds`, `AtWar`) plus a campaign day; `CampaignEvents.DailyTickEvent` broadcast plus a targeted join reply | Server |
| `special-resource-authority` (spend + balance) | false | `SpecialResourceSpendPolicy.ProtocolVersion = 2`; `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceBalanceV1.CurrentProtocolVersion = 1`; `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceSyncRequestV1.CurrentProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceSpendV2`, `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceBalanceV1`, `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceSyncRequestV1` | Client reports a measured spend with a session id and sequence; server applies it to its own balance and pushes back one targeted row per resource (`HeroId`, `ResourceId`, `Balance`, `Delta`, `Source`, `Sequence`) | Server |
| `server-gameplay-settings` | true | `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.TaomProtocolVersion = 3` | `NetworkTaomServerSettingsRequestV1`, `NetworkTaomServerSettingsV1` | 46 TAOM gameplay settings (`DefinitionArray = new ServerSettingDefinition[46]`) as flat `Keys[]` / `Values[]` string arrays, requested on the `CampaignReady` broker message | `taom-coop-settings.json`, but only conditionally (below) |
| `battle-size-ceiling` | true | `BattleSizeAuthorityPolicy.ProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkTaomBattleSizePolicyRequestV1`, `NetworkTaomBattleSizePolicyV1` | Battle-size index plus field, siege and sally-out troop counts | Server. The effective write is NOT the `BannerlordConfig` slot (below) |
| `elite-emissary-protocol` | true | `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseRequestV2.CurrentProtocolVersion = 2`, `...ResultV2 = 2`, `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceCaptureV2 = 2`, `...CaptureAckV2 = 2` | `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceCaptureV2`, `NetworkTaomSpecialResourceCaptureAckV2`, `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseRequestV2`, `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseResultV2` | Four-step elite troop purchase: resource capture, ack, purchase request, outcome carrying the authoritative balance | Server |
| `wotr-momentum-relay` | false | `NetworkWotrMomentumState.CurrentProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkWotrMomentumState` | The whole War of the Ring momentum store as a `Dictionary<string, string>`, broadcast on `CampaignEvents.HourlyTickEvent` | Server |
| `battle-spawn-reliability` | true | `BattleSpawnReliabilityProtocol.CurrentVersion = 1` | the four `NetworkBattleSpawn*` structs named above | Per-mission agent-transfer bookkeeping: receiver ready, replay announce, attempt ended, and the `ReadyAccepted` / `BatchSettled` / `TransferSettled` ack kinds | Peer-to-peer between mission controllers, over `IBattleNetwork` |
| `compressed-hero-join-transfer` | false | `NetworkCompressedNewPlayerHeroCreatedV1.CurrentProtocolVersion = 1` | `NetworkCompressedNewPlayerHeroCreatedV1` | A `GZipStream` (`CompressionLevel.Fastest`) envelope wrapping Coop's own `Coop.Core.Client.Services.Heroes.Messages.NetworkNewPlayerHeroCreated`, substituted by a Harmony prefix on `Coop.Core.Common.Network.CoopNetworkBase.SendAllBut(NetPeer, IMessage)` | The peer running the patched `SendAllBut` |

- [Verified] Two families the brief listed do not exist as separate protocols: `specialResourceSpend` and the balance/sync rows are one family owned by `special-resource-authority` and its client endpoint (three version literals inside it), and `heroStateCapture` has a type but no protocol (below).
- **`server-gameplay-settings` may never read a file.** [Verified] `ServerGameplaySettingsEndpoint.InitializeDedicatedServerPolicy()` returns `false` immediately when `BANNERLORD_USER_DIR` is unset, before any file check. Only when it is set does the endpoint look for `<BANNERLORD_USER_DIR>/taom-coop-settings.json` (`ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.FileName`), fall back to `Defaults()` with `no taom-coop-settings.json found; using packaged defaults`, or throw `InvalidDataException` on a malformed file. On false the component skips `BattleSizeAuthorityEndpoint.SetAuthoritativeServerPolicy` and logs `armed 39 server-owned TAOM simulation getters; awaiting server snapshot on clients`; all four `EnsureServerPolicy()` callers then install `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.Defaults()` on first use, so a host with no env var serves packaged defaults to clients as authoritative, indistinguishable on the wire from a configured one.
- **`battle-size-ceiling`'s `BannerlordConfig` write is not the live path under Coop.** [Verified] `WHAT-CHANGED.txt`: "Coop 0.1.4 reads its own reserve provider, so changing Bannerlord's old battle-size tables alone had no effect." The path that fires is `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter`, a transpiler on `Missions.Battles.BattleHostHandler.SendSideReserves` replacing the single `IBattleSizeProvider.GetBattleSize()` call with `ForMapEvent`, which reads `EnsureServerPolicy().BattleSize`. It is installed by **`server-gameplay-settings`**, not `battle-size-ceiling`, and gated on `TaomServerSettingsActivation.Enabled && !CompatState.Faulted && ModInformation.IsServer && mapEvent != null`; on any of those it returns `original.GetBattleSize()`, so a fault degrades this seam to Coop's own provider rather than killing it.
- **Position.** [Verified] `AuthoritativePositionCheckpointPolicy`: `IntervalSeconds = 1f`, `CorrectionThreshold = 0.5f`, plus a forward-movement protection window of `MaximumForwardProtectionSeconds = 20f` / `MaximumForwardProtectionDistance = 15f` that suppresses a correction while the client is genuinely moving toward the same point. [Verified] The **send** path is not gated by `AuthoritativePositionActivation` at all: `OnApplicationTick` tests the component's `_live` flag, `CompatState.Faulted` and `CoopRole.MayMutateSimulation` directly, and the activation static gates only the client receive path in `AuthoritativePositionClientHandler`. `CoopRole.MayMutateSimulation` is `!CompatState.Faulted && role == Role.Server && SessionActive() == true`; both the position and momentum senders pass it, so those two families carry a fault check independent of their activation static.
- **Battle size.** [Verified] `BattleSizeAuthorityPolicy.ServerDefault()` returns `new BattleSizeAuthoritySnapshot(1, 6, 2048, 2048, 800)`; `TopBattleSizeIndex = 6`, `MaximumBattleTroops = 2048`, and `CoopMaximumLiveAgents = 2000` is logged as unchanged by this policy. `SetTopEntry` runs against three fields (`_battleSizes`, `_siegeBattleSizes`, `_sallyOutBattleSizes`) and throws in three distinct cases: `MissingFieldException` when the field is absent or not a static `int[]`, `InvalidOperationException` "has no top slot" when `array == null || array.Length <= 6`, and `InvalidOperationException` "top index changed from 6 to N" when `array.Length - 1 != 6`. The packaged call passes `exact: false` and only raises the slot (`array[num] < value`); the server-confirmed call passes `exact: true`, overwrites, and `ApplyExact` additionally assigns `BannerlordConfig.BattleSize`. It fails loudly because both callers are unguarded: `ApplyPackagedCeiling()` runs inside `BattleSizeCeilingComponent.Install`, where a throw becomes a critical-component install failure and a module fault, and `ApplyExact` runs via `PrepareForMission` from the `DefaultBattleMissionAgentSpawnLogic` constructor prefix.
- **FullWar and Career.** [Verified] FullWar: `MaximumPairCount = 256`, `MaximumFactionIdLength = 128`, `MaximumRequestIdLength = 64`; `TryValidateSnapshot` rejects a pair count outside 1..256, mismatched array lengths, self-referential pairs, and duplicates using an order-independent `A\0B` key. Career: `MaxChoiceCount = 64`, `MaxTierCount = 3`, `MaxFlagCount = 64`, `MaxDetailLength = 256`, `MaxCareerIdLength = 128`, `MaxEntryIdLength = 128`, `MaxRequestIdLength = 64`; `CareerSnapshotFreshness.Evaluate` has five outcomes, not three: `Invalid` for anything but protocol 1 or a non-positive echoed client session id, server session id or sequence; `WrongClientSession` when the echoed **client** session id is not the one this client opened; `WrongServerSession` after pinning the first server session id it sees; `Stale` for any sequence at or below the high-water mark; otherwise `Accept`.
- **Special resource and emissary.** [Verified] `MaximumResourceIdLength = 128`, `MaximumSpend = 100000f`, `Epsilon = 0.001f`; the client session id is 63 bits (`BitConverter.ToInt64(...) & 0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFFL`, coerced to 1 if zero) from `RandomNumberGenerator`, regenerated per session, and the sync request gives up after `MaxSyncAttempts = 4`. The emissary client's resource capture keeps at most 16 entries (`dictionary.Count < 16`, rows past the 16th dropped with no log and no rejection), ids at most 128 chars with no `:`, values clamped by `Math.Min(value, 10000f)`, and throws `the local authenticated Coop hero could not be resolved` if `IControllerIdProvider` plus `IPlayerManager` plus `IObjectManager` cannot produce the hero. `EmissaryPurchasePolicy` carries the matching server caps: `MaxResourceEntries = 16`, `MaxResourceIdLength = 128`, `AbsoluteResourceCap = 10000f`, `MaxQuantity = 20`, `MaxRequestIdLength = 64`, `MaxSettlementIdLength = 128`, `MaxTroopIdLength = 128`.
- **Momentum.** [Verified] `MinRealSecondsBetweenSends = 60` and `KeepAliveHours = 12`. `OnHourlyTick` increments `_hoursSinceSend` first, then applies the 60-second wall-clock floor (`DateTime.UtcNow`), then the fingerprint check, so an unchanged store resends only once `_hoursSinceSend` reaches 12.
- **Battle spawn.** [Verified] `ReadyRetryInterval` 6s, `ReplayRetryInterval` 12s, `ReplayPhaseTimeout` 30s, `RequestTimeToLive` 10 minutes (all `TimeSpan` statics); `MaximumPeerRequests = 32`, `MaximumReadyAttempts = 3`, `MaximumReplayInvocations = 3`, `MaximumReplayInvocationsBeforeCommit = 2`, `MaximumTrackedTransfers = 16`, `MaximumBatchesPerTransfer = 128`, `MaximumRecordsPerBatch = 256`, `MaximumRecordsPerTransfer = 4096`, `MaximumTrackedAgentIdsPerBatch = 512`, `MaximumTrackedAgentIdsPerTransfer = 8192`. Correlation ids must satisfy `value.Length == 32 && Guid.TryParseExact(value, "N", out _)`.
- **Compressed hero, and settings coverage.** [Verified] `HeroJoinTransferCodec` engages only in a band: `MinimumOriginalBytes = 262144` (256 KiB) up to `MaximumOriginalBytes = MaximumCompressedBytes = 33554432` (32 MiB), and only when GZip lands under `MaximumUsefulRatio = 0.9`; anything outside falls through to Coop's original message, and `TryDecompress` re-enforces the band and aborts mid-stream past the declared `expectedOriginalBytes`. It is not a general envelope. The wire carries 46 setting definitions, but `TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent.Keys` patches 39 TAOM getters (counted entry by entry); the rest reach the game through `TaomPartySizeSettingsAdapter` and `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter`, which call `ServerGameplaySettingsEndpoint.TryGet*` directly rather than through a patched property.
- **Two declared message types that nothing sends.** [Verified] `NetworkTaomHeroStateCapture` (`CurrentProtocolVersion = 1`, carrying a capture sequence, career id, choices, tier unlocks, flags, a `Dictionary<string, float>` of special resources and a `FreshCharacterCreation` flag) and `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchase` (`CurrentProtocolVersion = 1`, four members: `ProtocolVersion`, `Sequence`, `TroopId`, `Count`) appear nowhere else in either decompiled tree: a word-boundary grep returns exactly two hits apiece, the class declaration and the constructor, both inside the declaring file. No `Subscribe<>`, no `Send`, no `SendAll`. They are superseded by `NetworkTaomEmissaryPurchaseRequestV2` and by the career family. [Inference] They read as name reservations, and if wire ids really are type names, removing them is not automatically free.

### Activation gating, faults, and the settings-version discrepancy

- **9 activation statics** in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Relay` (`*Activation.cs`). [Verified] Six are a single `Volatile` int, `Enabled` returning `!CompatState.Faulted && Volatile.Read(ref _enabled) == 1`: `AuthoritativePositionActivation`, `BattleSizeAuthorityActivation`, `CareerActivation`, `EmissaryPurchaseActivation`, `SpecialResourceActivation`, `TaomServerSettingsActivation`.
- [Verified] Two are tri-state, and on both the fault-checking accessor is the property `Current` (`SnapshotState` and `RelayState` are the nested enum **types**, not members): `FullWarDiplomacyActivation.Current` returns `SnapshotState.Closed` when faulted, `RelayActivation.Current` returns `RelayState.Closed`. Both enums are `{ Closed, Observe, Enabled }`, both open from the component's `ComponentMode`, but the openers differ: `Open(ComponentMode)` maps `mode != Enabled` to `Observe` (so `Off` would map to Observe), while `OpenMomentum(ComponentMode)` / `CloseMomentum()` uses an explicit `switch` (`Enabled` to 2, `Observe` to 1, anything else to 0).
- [Verified] "Observe logs what it would have applied and touches nothing" holds for **receivers only**: `WotrRelayClientHandler.HandleMomentum` logs `observe: would apply momentum broadcast #N (...); TAOM state untouched`, while the sender `WotrMomentumRelayBehavior.OnHourlyTick` requires `Current == RelayState.Enabled` and returns at the top with no log at all; FullWar's Observe passes into the handler and skips only the mutating step. [Verified] The `Off`-maps-to-`Observe` asymmetry is unreachable: `InstallAll` logs `<name> | off` and `continue`s before calling `Install` for any component resolved to `ComponentMode.Off`.
- [Verified] `BattleSpawnReliabilityActivation` is the ninth and is two-level: `CanOpenSession` carries the fault check plus the component flag, `Enabled` requires `CanOpenSession` plus a per-session flag, and `OpenSession()` itself re-tests `CanOpenSession`, so opening a session on a faulted module is a no-op. Members: `OpenComponent()` / `OpenSession()` / `CloseSession()` / `CloseAll()`.
- [Verified] There are **23** open/close call sites, not ten: 10 `Open*` and 13 `Close*`; 20 in `TAOM.CoopCompat.Components`, 3 in `Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat/BattleSpawnReliabilityClientHandler.cs` (`CloseAll` at 191 inside `FailClosed`, `OpenSession` at 267 on session start, `CloseSession` at 616 in `ClearSession`). Eight components follow the clean pairing, one open in `Install` and one close in `Deactivate`, accounting for 16 sites. Battle-spawn reliability breaks it four ways: `Install` (line 91) opens nothing and calls `CloseAll()` as its first statement; the open is `OpenComponent()` at line 126 in `OnGameFrameworkReady`, after six Missions seam patches are proven; `Deactivate` calls `CloseAll()` at 133; a fourth `CloseAll()` fires at line 244 from `RunContained`'s failure delegate, alongside `MarkFaulted("battle-spawn-reliability callback failed: " + operation)`. [Verified] `TaomServerSettingsActivation.Close()` is the only close that does extra work: it also calls `ServerGameplaySettingsEndpoint.ClearClientPolicy()`, nulling `_client` and `_pendingRequestId` under the endpoint lock.
- **`MarkFaulted` is one-way for the process and the first reason wins** (`Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref _reason, reason, null)`). [Verified] The first caller logs `FAULTED | <reason> | hosting, joining and campaign-ready are blocked for the rest of this process`; every later caller logs `FAULTED (already) | follow-on failure: <reason>`. So the reason on the `FAULTED` line is the first fault, not necessarily the one being investigated. Only `Reset()` clears it, and that also calls `ServerAttestation.ResetForTests()`.
- [Verified] There are **35** `MarkFaulted` call sites (36 grep hits, one being the declaration in `CompatState.cs`). Seven are in the handler and endpoint layer. Client-side: `BattleSizeAuthorityClientHandler.cs:53` `server battle-size policy was invalid or uncorrelated` (`TryValidate` failed, or `_pendingRequestId` did not match); `TaomServerSettingsClientHandler.cs:53` `server TAOM gameplay settings were invalid or uncorrelated`; `BattleSpawnReliabilityClientHandler.cs:196` `battle-spawn-reliability runtime callback failed: <operation>`; `EmissaryPurchaseClientEndpoint.cs:164` `elite-emissary result could not apply the server-authoritative resource balance`.
- [Verified] Three are server-side, so client-originated traffic can fault the host for every peer on it: `CareerAuthorityServerHandler.cs:298` `authoritative TAOM career row became malformed after mutation`; `CareerAuthorityServerHandler.cs:346` `TAOM rejected or malformed an authenticated fresh career claim`; `EmissaryPurchaseServerHandler.cs:482` `elite-emissary purchase failed after a destructive write and rollback could not be proven`. An eighth, wire-adjacent: `BattleSizeCeilingComponent.cs:95` logs, calls `MarkFaulted("server battle-size authority did not reach spawn logic")` and throws if the constructed spawn logic did not take the server value.
- [Verified] The emissary client fault is narrower than its reason string suggests: `Accept` first drops any result with no matching pending request (`elite-emissary-protocol | ignored unmatched server result`, warn only), and the balance write-back runs only when `HeroId` and `ResourceId` are non-empty and `AuthoritativeBalance` is finite and `>= 0f`. The `Math.Abs(...) > 0.001f` read-back check faults only inside that window.
- **One family is not gated at all.** [Verified] `compressed-hero-join-transfer` has no activation static (grep for `Activation` in `CompressedHeroJoinTransferComponent.cs` returns nothing) and is `Critical => false`. Its receive path checks four things, none of them the fault flag: `ProtocolVersion != 1`, a bounded `HeroJoinTransferCodec.TryDecompress`, a `Type.FullName` equality test against `Coop.Core.Client.Services.Heroes.Messages.NetworkNewPlayerHeroCreated` on the restored message, and `payload.Who is NetPeer`; all four failures share one `LogInvalid` that fires once per process (`Interlocked.Exchange(ref _invalidLogged, 1)`). Its send path, the `SendAllBut` prefix, tests the target message's type name, the codec band and `_mode`, never `CompatState.Faulted`. [Verified] It can also half-install: `TryInstall` sets `_gaveUp` and logs `exact SendAllBut/serializer seam changed; Coop's original Hero broadcast remains active`, leaving the receiver subscribed and the sender unpatched, and because `Critical => false` nothing faults.
- **A runtime fault unpatches nothing.** [Verified] `ComponentRegistry.RollBackAll` has exactly five call sites, all at module startup: `ComponentRegistry.cs:107` (end of the install loop when faulted), `:132` (the `CompatState.Faulted` guard in `OnGameFrameworkReady`), `:152` (a critical framework-ready failure), plus `CoopCompatSubModule.cs:611` (session-guard install failure) and `:629` (a throwing framework-ready hook). `MarkFaulted` never calls it, so every installed prefix stays attached.
- [Verified] What happens instead: the nine gated families go silent on their next check, and `CoopCompatSubModule.OnApplicationTick` calls `TearDownFaultedSession()` first thing every frame. It returns immediately unless `CompatState.Faulted` is set, the session was not already torn down, and `ServerAttestation.ServingFaultHardStopRegistered` is false; otherwise it calls `JoinRefusal.TearDownLiveSession()`, which reflects `Dispose()` onto the Coop experience and then verifies `Running`, the `container` field and `CoopRole.SessionActive()`. Anything unconfirmed is `Retry` (throttled to roughly one attempt per second); five failed attempts throws; a partial teardown returns `TerminallyUnsafe` and throws.
- [Verified] On success it sets `_sessionTornDown`, calls `JoinRefusal.MarkRestartRequired()` and logs `session teardown | complete: cancellation signalled, network disposed and the container released. This peer is no longer participating.
