RESTART THE GAME: Coop's IGameInterface.UnpatchAll is a no-op in this build, so its Harmony patches are still attached to live game methods — inert only while no container exists.` `JoinRefusal.Install` has already prefixed `StartAsClient` and `StartAsServer` on `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience` with `RefuseWhileFaulted`, which returns `false` (skipping the original) once faulted or restart-required, and `EnforceFaultAtCampaignStart` throws at campaign start on the same two conditions. [Verified] `TryUnpatch` remains the path that can fault again: `harmony.UnpatchAll(harmony.Id)` inside a try, scoped to that component's own Harmony id rather than globally, and on throw `MarkFaulted(name + ": partial patches could not be rolled back")`. Two regimes, then: faulted with no live session is a quiet nine-family blackout; faulted mid-session is a session teardown plus a required process restart.
- **`taomServerSettings`: three artifacts, two answers.** [Verified] `PACKAGE.json` `protocols.taomServerSettings` says `2`; `PACKAGE.json` `knownOpen[0]` says "0.3.15 changes TAOM settings protocol to 3; all peers must update. Actual model/handler fixture checks pass in both roles, while new in-game acceptance remains pending"; `WHAT-CHANGED.txt` line 5 says "Every player and the host must use Compat 0.3.15: TAOM settings protocol is now 3."
- [Verified] The assembly is the tiebreaker and says 3, in five independent places: `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.TaomProtocolVersion = 3`; `NetworkTaomServerSettingsV1`'s constructor assigns `ProtocolVersion = 3`; `NetworkTaomServerSettingsRequestV1`'s constructor assigns `ProtocolVersion = 3`; `TaomServerSettingsServerHandler.HandleRequest` drops the request with `server-gameplay-settings | ignored malformed TAOM policy request` when `what.ProtocolVersion != 3`, the request id is empty, or it is longer than 64; and `ServerGameplaySettingsPolicy.TryValidateWireSnapshot` returns `"unsupported TAOM settings protocol"` for anything but 3. The `protocols` block is stale for this one key; its other four entries match the assembly (`authoritativePartyPosition: 2`, `careerAuthority: 1`, `fullWarDiplomacy: 1`, `specialResourceSpend: 2`).
- [Verified] That block covers 5 families of 10: `battle-size-ceiling`, `elite-emissary-protocol`, `wotr-momentum-relay`, `battle-spawn-reliability` and `compressed-hero-join-transfer` have no `protocols` entry, so the manifest can neither go stale nor warn on them. The manifest also declares `"allPeersRequireExactCompat": true` and `"launcherPolicy": "exact-supported-tuple"`, with `launcherOrder` pinning `CoopNightly` then `TAOM.CoopCompat`. [Inference] If the launcher enforces that tuple, a protocol-2 peer never reaches a 0.3.15 host and the stale key is a documentation defect rather than a live hazard; the launcher code is not in the evidence set, so that is not established here.
- [Verified] Where it is reachable, the client half of the failure is quiet. A request whose version is not 3 is dropped by the host with one `Warn` and no reply, and with no log at all when `TaomServerSettingsActivation.Enabled` is false or `payload.Who` is not a `NetPeer`. A client that never gets a reply keeps `_pendingRequestId` set and `_client` null; `ServerGameplaySettingsEndpoint.CurrentTaomPolicy()` returns `_server?.Taom ?? _client`, and `_server` stays null on a client (`InitializeDedicatedServerPolicy` returns false without `BANNERLORD_USER_DIR`, and the only other reachable `EnsureServerPolicy` call outside the server handlers, `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter.ForMapEvent`, is gated on `ModInformation.IsServer`), so every `TryGetBoolean` / `TryGetInteger` / `TryGetFloat` returns `false` with a zero out-value.
- [Verified] The consequence is **not** zeroed settings: `OverrideBoolean`, `OverrideInteger` and `OverrideFloat` all `return true` on that path, which runs the original TAOM getter, so the peer silently plays on its own local values. Reading the zero out-value as the effective setting would be wrong. There is no fault, no disconnect and no player-visible message.
- [Verified] The quiet path is bounded to non-receipt. A policy that IS received and then fails `AcceptClientPolicy` is loud: the client logs `server-gameplay-settings | rejected authoritative TAOM policy: <reason>` and calls `MarkFaulted("server TAOM gameplay settings were invalid or uncorrelated")`, which under a live session triggers the teardown above. Both client paths are additionally gated on `ModInformation.IsClient`. [Inference] Which half an old peer gets cannot be settled from this assembly: a settings message arriving at version 2 would fault the client rather than run silently, but whether an older host replies at all depends on the old build's own request gate, which is not in evidence, so the claim that the break is symmetric is unproven here.
- **`coop-taom.json` carries two config gates, neither a wire protocol, and they are not equally strict.** [Verified] `schemaVersion` is mandatory: a missing key, a non-integer, or any value but 1 adds `coop-taom.json: missing or unsupported schemaVersion (expected integer 1)`. `protocolVersion` is optional (`CompatConfig.ProtocolVersion` is `int?`, checked only inside `if (dictionary.TryGetValue("protocolVersion", out var value2))` at `CompatConfig.cs:164`): a non-integer adds `protocolVersion must be an integer`, anything but 1 adds `coop-taom.json: protocolVersion <n> does not match this build's protocol v1`. Either failure fails the whole registry: `InstallAll` logs each error, calls `MarkFaulted("configuration invalid — all components off")` and returns before the install loop, so no component is ever constructed. [Verified] `PeerIdentity.ProtocolVersion = 1` is a further number again, used only to build the peer stamp `TAOM.CoopCompat <version> | protocol v1 | sha256 <16 hex>`.

## The TAOM binding surface: what a rename breaks

`TAOM.CoopCompat.csproj` carries `<Reference Include="TAOM" />`, one of 16 references (the others: `Common`, `LiteNetLib`, `GameInterface`, `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem`, `TaleWorlds.Library`, `Serilog`, `Missions`, `System.Core`, `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade`, `TaleWorlds.Core`, `protobuf-net.Core`, `0Harmony`, `System.Xml`, `TaleWorlds.Localization`, `TaleWorlds.ObjectSystem`), so half the surface is a hard compile-time link and half is string reflection through `AccessTools.TypeByName` / `Type.GetType`. Both halves break on a rename, at different moments, usually with the same end state. [Verified]
**`Coop.Core` is NOT a compile-time reference** despite `using Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat;` in `CareerAuthorityComponent`: the compat declares its own types in that namespace and the decompile emits `main/Coop.Core.Client.Services.TaomCoopCompat/` as a namespace folder of this assembly. Coop is reached only by reflection and FullName/MVID string comparison. [Verified: no `Coop.Core` in the reference list]
The TAOM link is **30 `using TAOM.<non-CoopCompat>` directives across 14 files and 11 namespaces** (`TAOM.Features.SpecialResources` x7, `TAOM.Features.SpecialResources.Domain` x5, `TAOM.Adapters` x4, `TAOM.Features.CareerSystem` x3, plus 7 more); the bootstrap assembly carries none. The string `CoopCompat` appears in no `.cs`, `.md` or `.xml` anywhere in the TAOM repo, so nothing on TAOM's side knows this surface exists. [Verified: per-directive and per-file grep over both trees; repo-wide grep returns nothing]
**Failure model.** `main/TAOM.CoopCompat.Components/` holds **100 `.cs` files, 83 referencing `ICompatComponent`**, 25 declaring `Critical => true`; `CoopCompatSubModule.OnSubModuleLoad` makes 78 `_registry.Add` calls (lines 186 to 263) then one `_registry.InstallAll()` (`CoopCompatSubModule.cs:174-185`), so the verdict is computed at module load, before any co-op intent. **Five of the 83 implementations are dead** (`ArmyRegistrationRepairComponent`, `BattleSizeCeilingComponent`, `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent`, `BattleUplinkGuardComponent`, `LocationNpcEquipmentSamplingGuardComponent` are instantiated nowhere in either tree); three are critical, so the **live critical count is 22, not 25**, and the `Missions` MVID pin in Table 3 never executes. [Verified: `grep -c "_registry.Add("` = 78; per-name `new <Name>(` grep across `main/` and `boot/` empty for all five]
`ComponentRegistry.InstallAll` (the method is `InstallAll`; there is no `ComponentRegistry.Install`) walks `_components` in registration order, wrapping each `component.Install` in try/catch; on a throw it logs, unpatches that component's own Harmony instance, deactivates it, and calls `CompatState.MarkFaulted` **only if `SafeCritical(component)`**. A non-critical throw disables exactly one component and nothing else. [Verified: `ComponentRegistry.cs:61-104`, catch at 94, `MarkFaulted` at 101]

| Guard | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Registration order decides which break you see** | Every iteration re-checks `CompatState.Faulted` first, logging `"NOT installed: the module already faulted earlier in this pass"` and skipping. The first critical failure wins; everything downstream never runs. [Verified: `ComponentRegistry.cs:63-67`] |
| **`RollBackAll` is not "all 78"** | It runs ONCE after the loop, not at the throw, and iterates `_live`, which holds only components that completed `Install`. A fault at position N rolls back at most N-1. [Verified: `ComponentRegistry.cs:105-108`, `111-123`] |
| **The `coop-taom.json` kill switch cannot rescue a critical component** | `ModeFor` is consulted first, and a critical component requested as anything but `Enabled` calls `MarkFaulted` on the spot: `critical component "{name}" must be enabled (requested {mode})`. An invalid config is worse: every component is skipped and the module faults with `configuration invalid`. No operator escape hatch exists for a broken critical binding. [Verified: `ComponentRegistry.cs:52-60`, `74-79`] |
| **A fault blocks co-op, not single-player** | `EnforceFaultAtCampaignStart` returns early unless `CompatState.Faulted` **and** `CoopRole.SessionActive() != false`; only then does it throw `"TAOM.CoopCompat is faulted and refuses to start a co-op campaign: "` plus the reason. With every patch rolled back, single-player runs on unmodified TAOM. Caveat: `SessionActive()` is `bool?` and yields `null` when `GameInterface.ContainerProvider.TryGetContainer` cannot be resolved, and `null` still throws. [Verified: `CoopCompatSubModule.cs:589,595`; `CoopRole.SessionActive`] |

If a session is live when the fault lands, `OnApplicationTick` tears it down through `JoinRefusal.TearDownLiveSession()`, calls `JoinRefusal.MarkRestartRequired()` and refuses every further campaign entry in that process, because Coop's `IGameInterface.UnpatchAll` is a no-op in this build so its Harmony patches survive its own teardown. [Verified: teardown switch `CoopCompatSubModule.cs:562-573` and the log text at 569; the `UnpatchAll` behaviour is the compat author's assertion, not read out of the Coop assembly]
**Table 1: every TAOM identifier bound by name.** **C** marks a binding held by a `Critical => true` component; for every **C** row the traced consequence is a module fault plus a blocked or torn-down co-op session, **not** feature-level degradation. The compat has no partial-failure mode for critical bindings. Seams (`TaomEmissarySeam`, `TaomMomentumSeam`, `TaomPartySizeSettingsAdapter`) are static helpers with no `Critical` member; criticality is the installing component's. Rows merge identifiers sharing a binding shape and consequence; no binding is omitted.

**`CoopPatchShieldComponent` is not a component.** It is a `public static class` with no `Name` / `Critical` / `DefaultMode`, never passed to `_registry.Add`, invoked as `CoopPatchShieldComponent.TryInstallEarly()` from the `CoopCompatSubModule` constructor (line 171) and again at the top of `OnSubModuleLoad` (line 180). Its entire body sits inside one `try { … } catch (Exception ex) { _status = "WARNING: early co-op policy unavailable: " … }` (lines 14 to 52), returning early with `"no selected Coop/CoopNightly module; no changes"` when neither Coop module is active. Renaming `CoopPresence` or `PatchShield` makes `TypeByName` return null, `CoopPatchShieldPolicy.Install` throws `MissingMethodException("TAOM co-op presence/PatchShield API changed")` on the null-propagated `ReturnType` check, and that exception is swallowed into a status string printed with `Console.WriteLine` and later logged at Info. No fault, no rollback, no session refusal, and the registry's mode system cannot reach it. Both types also live in the **separate `TAOM.Dependencies` assembly** (`Dependencies/Foundation/CoopPresence.cs`, `Dependencies/Foundation/PatchShield.cs`), which the pinned TAOM MVID in Table 3 does not cover. [Verified]

**Table 2: the settings-getter surface.** `TaomServerSettingsAuthorityComponent` (`Critical => true`, line 66) holds one `IReadOnlyDictionary<string,string> Keys` with **exactly 39 entries** (lines 21 to 59) mapping `"<Namespace>.<Type>.<Property>"` to a wire key. Per entry, `Install` does `AccessTools.TypeByName(typeName) ?? throw new TypeLoadException`, `AccessTools.DeclaredProperty(...) ?? throw new MissingMemberException`, `GetGetMethod(nonPublic: true) ?? throw new MissingMethodException`, rejects any property whose type is not `bool` / `int` / `float` (`throw new InvalidOperationException(key + " has unsupported return type")`, line 108), Harmony-patches the getter, then **proves** the patch landed (`Prove(...)` throws `settings getter patch could not be proven` if `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` does not report it). All 39 composites plus `IsAvailable` resolve against HEAD and all 40 properties are `bool`, `int` or `float`. A single `RequireAssembly(assembly, fullName, mvid, label)` helper exists, in `SiegeDefenseTelemetryComponent.cs:139-145`, with exactly two call sites (102, 122). Assembly `FullName` literals are a weaker pin, carrying only the frozen `AssemblyVersion`. `main/` counts: `"TAOM, Version=2.0.0.0, Culture=neutral, PublicKeyToken=null"` 6 (`FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent` x2, `SiegeDefenseTelemetryComponent` x2, `TroopWeightPolicyComponent` x2), `GameInterface, Version=1.0.0.0` 17, `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem, Version=1.0.0.0` 13, `Coop.Core, Version=1.0.0.0` 8, `Missions, Version=1.0.0.0` 3 (`BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent.cs:73,167,169`), `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade, Version=1.0.0.0` 3 (`SiegeDestructibleStateGuardComponent.cs:16,133,156`). `boot/` holds 1 each of `GameInterface`, `Coop.Core`, `Missions`, `LiteNetLib, Version=1.3.1.0`, `Common, Version=1.0.0.0`, `protobuf-net.Core, Version=3.0.0.0`, `protobuf-net, Version=3.0.0.0`, and zero TAOM: `boot/TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap/CoopDependencyBridgePolicy.cs` holds a `CoopDependencyPin[7]` array carrying those 7 FullNames alongside byte sizes and SHA-256 hashes, referencing no TAOM type and no TAOM literal, so nothing in `boot/` belongs to the TAOM binding surface. So `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.ResolveExactTaomType`, whose failure message reads `"does not belong to the exact TAOM 2.0.20 contract"` (line 145), verifies nothing version-specific and passes on v2.0.20 and v2.0.28 alike. **The MVID check is assembly-wide, it already fails, and it is not the fault that fires.** `Main/TAOM.csproj:5-6` sets `<Deterministic>true</Deterministic>` with `<PathMap>$(MSBuildProjectDirectory)=/_/</PathMap>`, but [Verified] `Directory.Build.props:23-24` bakes a `UtcNow` build stamp into `InformationalVersion`, so the MVID moves on every build as well as with the content of the compilation; `Main/TAOM.csproj` is the single project producing `TAOM.dll` with default compile items, so a change to any C# file under `Main/` moves it whether or not `FieldCampOverlayVM` was touched (`Dependencies/TAOM.Dependencies.csproj` builds a separate assembly the pin does not cover). Both `TAOM.dll` builds on disk miss the pinned `bd1af67e-d002-47d1-bf58-83e8967fd143`: `Main/bin/x64/Debug/net472/TAOM.dll` (2026-09-06 23:13:59, 3,094,528 bytes, MVID `40f9ed03-8d62-4f01-a608-a4cb9b02083f`) and `Main/bin/Debug/net472/TAOM.dll` (2026-09-07 10:46:09, 3,104,768 bytes, MVID `b5f89f5b-df46-42ee-9f18-204d685ce728`). **The headline, confirmed, and it lands before the MVID pin does.** `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.ResolveExactSizePenalty` filters declared public instance methods named `ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty`, asserts exactly one match, then rejects on a **disjunction of seven failure conditions** (two drafts printed it as a five-term conjunction of success conditions, which is not what the source says): `methodInfo.IsStatic`, `IsGenericMethod`, `ReturnType != typeof(void)`, `parameters.Length != 2`, `parameters[0].ParameterType != typeof(PartyBase)`, `!parameters[1].ParameterType.IsByRef`, `parameters[1].ParameterType.GetElementType()?.FullName != "TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem.ExplainedNumber"`. `parameters.Length` is now 3, so the guard throws `MissingMethodException("TAOM.Features.TroopWeight.TroopWeightService", "public void ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty(PartyBase, ref ExplainedNumber)")`. The component is `Critical => true` and 13th of 78, so this is the first TAOM binding failure the registry reaches. Whether components 1 through 12 install cleanly on a given machine is a separate question: several pin the `GameInterface` and `Coop.Core` MVIDs, and one of those failing first would move the reported cause off TAOM entirely [Inference]. [Verified: `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs:24`, predicate at 129, throw at 131; `Main/Features/TroopWeight/TroopWeightService.cs:103` at HEAD reads `public void ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty(PartyBase party, ref ExplainedNumber limit, bool includeDescriptions)`; `ITroopWeightService.cs:25` at `v2.0.26` carried the 2-parameter form]
The TAOM change is a good one and TAOM had no way to know it was load-bearing: the new parameter carries vanilla's display/enforcement discriminator so the tooltip stops rendering a negative "Heavy troops" line, with the cost moved to the used side via `TroopWeightDisplay`. The compat's prefix is `private static bool SkipSizePenalty()`, a parameterless method that unconditionally returns `false` (`TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs:187-190`), so it never wanted the original body to run in co-op at all and its own shape is arity-independent. An arity-tolerant lookup would have kept working; the exact-arity assertion is what turns an additive change into a session-blocking fault. [Verified on both code paths and on the interface's own doc comment; Inference on intent and on the arity-tolerant counterfactual]
Two semantic drifts break no binding [Inference, reasoned from the diffs and from the compat's interception tables being closed sets; not observed in a live session]: `CareerDataService.ResetForNewCampaign()` is a new mutation path `CareerAuthorityComponent` does not prefix, its eight prefixes (`PrefixAdd`, `PrefixRemove`, `PrefixSwitch`, `PrefixSetCareer`, `PrefixClearCareer`, `PrefixUnlockTier`, `PrefixSetFlag`, `PrefixTierGate`) plus one postfix (`PostfixRefresh`) being the complete set [Verified: the nine `harmony.Patch` calls at `CareerAuthorityComponent.cs:98-106`], so a peer that raises `OnNewGameCreatedEvent` replaces its local career dictionary with no request to the server and no snapshot invalidation; and `AiPartySizeService.ApplyCaravanScaling` is absent from `TaomPartySizeSettingsAdapter`'s four-entry table, so **while a server snapshot is live** caravan caps stay client-local while lord and garrison caps are server-owned (outside a live snapshot `Active()` lets all four originals run, so the asymmetry does not exist).
**The work item.** `.claude/skills/verify-bindings/SKILL.md` plus `docs/reference/taleworlds-api-snapshot/` (`README.md`, `reflection-sites.md`, `patch-targets.md`, `gamemodel-bases.md`), backed by six files in `TAOM.Tests/Migration/` (`GameModelOverrideBindingTests.cs`, `HarmonyPatchBindingTests.cs`, `ReflectionSiteBindingTests.cs`, `HarmonyFieldInjectionNamingTests.cs`, plus the `GameAssemblies.cs` and `IlCallScanner.cs` helpers), already turn a silent binding failure into a red `dotnet test`. But the snapshot's stated scope is outbound: "the exact TaleWorlds v1.4.8 engine surface that TAOM patches, overrides, and reflects into." Nothing covers the inbound direction, where a third party reflects into TAOM. [Verified: the skill directory, the snapshot README's opening line, the migration test listing, the repo-wide `CoopCompat` grep returning nothing] The gap is one file wide. A `CoopCompatBindingTests` in `TAOM.Tests/Migration/`, seeded from Table 1 and Table 2 as `[DataRow]`s and resolving each member out of the built `TAOM.dll` exactly as `AccessTools` would, costs a few hundred lines and makes the `ApplyPartySizeWeightPenalty` class of break visible at commit time instead of at a player's session-join. Prioritise the bindings held by the 22 LIVE critical components, and order the rows by registration index so the test reports the break the registry would actually hit first. The MVID pins cannot be tested that way and should not be: they are a design choice on the compat author's side guaranteeing rebuild-for-rebuild lockstep, and the honest fix there is a versioned compat contract rather than a test.

## Harmony patches the compat declares

**Owner ids.** [Verified] Exactly three `new Harmony(` sites exist. `ComponentRegistry.cs:85` builds `"taom.coopcompat." + component.Name` fresh per component, and that instance is the rollback unit: `TryUnpatch` calls `harmony.UnpatchAll(harmony.Id)` for only that component on an install throw (line 97), a framework-ready throw (line 147) or any fault (line 119), leaving every other component attached. The other two ids are `taom.coopcompat.join-refusal` (`CoopCompatSubModule.cs:545`, `SessionGuardHarmonyId` declared line 17, guarding `Coop.Core.CoopartiveMultiplayerExperience::StartAsClient` / `::StartAsServer`) and `taom.coopcompat.coop-patchshield-policy` (`CoopPatchShieldPolicy.cs:41`, const `Owner` line 10, guarding TAOM's `CoopPresence` / `PatchShield`). `ComponentRegistry.HarmonyIdPrefix` (const `"taom.coopcompat"`, line 17) is declared and never referenced: the id comes from a separate literal, so renaming the const changes no owner id. Case-insensitive duplicate `Name`s are rejected before any install (`InstallAll` lines 39-46) via `CompatState.MarkFaulted`. 83 components declare a `Name`, 62 patch; `TAOM.CoopCompat.Bootstrap` holds no `.Patch(` at all.

**All imperative, zero attributes.** [Verified] No `[HarmonyPatch]` / `HarmonyPrefix` / `HarmonyPostfix` / `HarmonyTranspiler` / `HarmonyFinalizer` attribute anywhere, and no `PatchAll`; the one grep hit is `CoopPatchShieldPolicy.cs:51`'s `val.Unpatch(methodBase, (HarmonyPatchType)0, "TAOM.Dependencies.Foundation.PatchShield")`, where [Inference] `0` is `HarmonyPatchType.All`. Everything goes through the five-arg positional `harmony.Patch(...)`: **101** call sites (78 on the registry-supplied `harmony` parameter, 23 on a cached `_harmony` field) across **66** files (62 named components + 2 static settings adapters + `CoopPatchShieldPolicy` + `JoinRefusal`), holding **117** non-null patch-method slots (67 prefix, 31 postfix, 11 transpiler, 8 finalizer). [Verified] Both are static slot counts, not runtime attachments: six sites expand inside a `foreach` (`server-gameplay-settings` 39 property getters from one site, `battle-order-timing-probe` at least 8, `settlement-leave-routing` up to 4, `control-mode-view-removal-guard` up to 2, `TaomPartySizeSettingsAdapter` exactly 4, `join-refusal` once per resolved overload), and `BattleSpawnReliabilityComponent.PatchAndVerify` (one `.Patch(` at line 153 carrying a prefix and a postfix slot) is called six times with a different mix: one prefix, five postfixes. No runtime attachment count is derivable statically.

**11 transpilers, 7 files** (six named components plus `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter`, which declares no `Name` and runs under `server-gameplay-settings`'s Harmony instance). `deterministic-battle-scene` (**Critical**) swaps the vendor's random scene pick in `FieldBattleMissionInitializer::Create` and `::GetBattleSceneForMapPatch` for a server-shared `RandomTerrainSeed`, and install throws unless `createRewrites == 1 && pickerRewrites == 1` plus both owned-transpiler counts. `raid-loot-isolation` (**Critical**) redirects loot into a temporary `ItemRoster` over four targets (two private `RaidEventComponent` members, each accepting either of two IL SHA256s for the client and server variants; `RaidEventComponentPatches::GetAddedItems` `D45E9807…`; `RaidProductionRewardsHandler::ApplyLootedItems` `211B2271…`). The rest: `donate-prisoner-garrison-guard` swaps `Settlement.AddGarrisonParty` for an authority-gated call in `PartyScreenHelper::OpenScreenAsDonatePrisoners`; `location-npc-equipment-sampling-guard` samples the per-agent equipment capture in `AgentMovementHandler::PollMovementAgents`; `hero-home-settlement-diagnostic-coalescer` (`HeroFieldPatches::HomeSettlementIntercept`) and `equipment-diagnostic-coalescer` (`AutoSync.Equipment_DynamicPatches::_equipmentType_Intercept`) replace vendor error sinks with sampled emitters; `CoopBattleSizeSettingsAdapter` rewrites the battle-size read in `BattleHostHandler::SendSideReserves`.

[Verified] None of the 11 is also a TAOM transpiler target. TAOM's nine hit `Banner.TryGetBannerDataFromCode`, `CampaignSceneNotificationHelper.CreateNotificationCharacter`, `AiVisitSettlementBehavior.AiHourlyTick`, `AiVisitSettlementBehavior.FillSettlementsToVisitWithDistancesAsDays`, `BodyGeneratorView.RefreshCharacterEntityAux`, `BehaviorComponent.OnBehaviorActivated`, `RomanceCampaignBehavior.CheckNpcMarriages`, `MobilePartyVisual.AddCharacterToPartyIcon` and `HeroCreator.DeliverOffSpring`: disjoint sets, so no method in the process carries a transpiler from both mods. Triage trap: the compat transpiles `GameInterface.Services.MapEvents.FieldBattleMissionInitializer::GetBattleSceneForMapPatch`, while TAOM's `BattleSceneSelection_Patch` (`Patch43_BattleLoadDiagnostics`) postfixes `DefaultSceneModel.GetBattleSceneForMapPatch`. Same method name, different declaring types, no interaction.

**8 finalizers, 8 files, all containment.** [Verified] Five swallow or observe on a vendor or engine teardown path (`control-mode-view-removal-guard`, `tournament-agent-removal-guard`, `recruitment-volunteer-null-guard`, `encounter-attack-condition-guard`, `mission-after-start-failure-probe`); three are scope-exit partners to a prefix/postfix pair that must unwind on a throw (`encounter-map-event-recovery`, `join-baseline-reconciliation`, `nested-kingdom-decision-deferral`). `SwallowTeardownNullReference`, `SwallowParticipantNullReference` and `ContainStaleAttack` test `if (!(__exception is NullReferenceException)) return __exception;` directly; `recruitment-volunteer-null-guard` routes the same decision through `RecruitmentVolunteerNullGuardPolicy.ShouldSuppress(active, isServer, __exception)`. `ContainStaleAttack` narrows to a client inside a live co-op session and rewrites `__result` plus the menu tooltip before returning `null`. `RecordFailure(Mission __instance, Exception __exception)` is read-only: it logs and returns `__exception` unchanged on every path including its own catch, so it never absorbs the fault. That matters because TAOM's `Mission_AfterStart_BattleLoad_Patch` (`Patch43_BattleLoadDiagnostics`) already carries a Prefix and a Postfix on the same `Mission.AfterStart`.

**Verification discipline TAOM does not have.** [Verified] 39 of the 66 patching files call `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` after patching and throw when the patch did not attach; the helpers are named inconsistently (`RequireOwnedExactPatch`, `RequireOwnedPatch`, `VerifyOwned`, `Prove`, `PatchAndVerify`, `CountOwnedTranspilers`, `IsGuardedBy`) but all re-read Harmony's list and fail the install unless exactly one entry matches this owner id and this exact patch method (canonical form: `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.cs:150-158`, throwing `InvalidOperationException("Harmony did not report exact troop-weight …")`). `ComponentRegistry` then rolls that component back and faults the module if it is `Critical`. `basic-character-skill-load-guard` re-verifies at framework-ready that its own prefix is attached exactly once and the qualified Coop prefix count is at most one. TAOM instead binds `[HarmonyPatch]` attributes through `PatchAll`, where a failed resolve is a silent no-op caught only at build time by the `Patch*BindingTests`; the compat proves attachment in the running process and turns the no-op into a loud rollback.

- **MVID pins** (assembly `FullName` + `ModuleVersionId`, checked before patching): `3c2da906-f6ec-443d-afa8-aad1c8286c03` `GameInterface, Version=1.0.0.0` (17 references), `886629fe-6e60-40d7-9a57-8d46017179d9` `TaleWorlds.CampaignSystem, Version=1.0.0.0` (11), `03ff70df-eadc-46c9-a51e-b68e8c3336ef` `Coop.Core, Version=1.0.0.0` (4), `bd1af67e-d002-47d1-bf58-83e8967fd143` `TAOM, Version=2.0.0.0` (4), `4267abec-e4ba-4746-94b7-47cbe3ba79f8` `TaleWorlds.MountAndBlade, Version=1.0.0.0` client (2), `1bcf228e-dc3e-4af3-8e86-8d749905f4c2` its server variant (2), `66f75064-7cf2-4bd5-b83c-d286de3e2f77` `Missions, Version=1.0.0.0` (1).
- **IL SHA256 pins:** `365686E1…` `Coop.Core.Server.CoopServer::<Update>b__22_0` (compiler-generated), `F043B661…` `ObjectManager::Remove(object)`, `8A553CD3…` `OverloadedPeerManager::ResumeTime(DateTime)`, `1E431784…` `HeroFieldPatches::HomeSettlementIntercept`, plus 6 constants over `raid-loot-isolation`'s 4 targets.
- **IL byte-length pins:** 574 `RecruitmentCampaignBehavior::UpdateVolunteersOfNotablesInSettlement`, 1072 `DefaultPartyTrainingModel::GetEffectiveDailyExperience`, 887 `DefaultPartyWageModelInterface::GetTotalWage`, 159 `PartyBase::OnXpChanged`, 295 / 54 the two `FieldCampOverlayVM` seams.
- **The pin is not universal, and one message is stale.** `TroopWeightPolicyComponent.ResolveExactTaomType` checks the TAOM assembly `FullName` only, no MVID, and names the "exact TAOM **2.0.20** contract" while `FieldCampCoopSuppressionComponent` and `SiegeDefenseTelemetryComponent` pin `bd1af67e-…` and say 2.0.26. The assembly's `AssemblyInformationalVersion` is `0.3.15-taom-2.0.26-authoritative-party-settings`, so 2.0.20 is a stale string, not a second contract.
- **The gap:** of the 27 files skipping the post-patch check most are probes that log and stand down (`kingdom-decision-probe`, `battle-order-timing-probe`, `settlement-leave-routing`, `puppet-initial-wield`), but `army-registration-repair` and `facegen` both declare `Critical => true`, and `army-registration-repair` throws `MissingMethodException` / `MissingMemberException` on a seam mismatch while never confirming its own prefix attached.

**One true same-method collision:** `Kingdom::AddDecision(KingdomDecision, bool)`. TAOM's `Patch70_FiefGrantDecisionSwap` runs `[HarmonyPrefix] public static void Prefix(ref KingdomDecision kingdomDecision)` at default priority, swapping in `TaomSettlementClaimantDecision`; the compat's `nested-kingdom-decision-deferral` runs `DeferNestedDecision(Kingdom, KingdomDecision, bool)` at `priority = 800` and **returns `false`** when the server is inside a `CoopKingdomElection`. [Verified] Both are live on the same peer: TAOM's prefix early-returns only when `ICoopSessionProvider.ShouldDeferToHost` (the client case) and the compat's deferral is gated on `Common.ModInformation.IsServer`, so on the host both attach and the compat runs first. Scopes differ too: TAOM swaps only a `SettlementClaimantDecision` that is not already a `TaomSettlementClaimantDecision`, the compat defers any `KingdomDecision` at election depth above zero.

[Verified] The decision is not dropped: `OnApplicationTick` (only when `_electionDepth == 0`, `ModInformation.IsServer`, live session) dequeues `Pending` and calls `pendingDecision.Kingdom.AddDecision(...)` again with the guard now returning `true`; the queue caps at 256 (`MaxPendingDecisions`) with a fall-through to the synchronous path once full, and `ClearPending()` discards on deactivate. [Inference] On the host a fief-grant decision raised inside a Coop election is delayed at least one frame and TAOM's swap lands on the replay, not the original call. Whether TAOM's void prefix also runs on the suppressed first pass depends on the loaded HarmonyLib's skip-remaining-prefixes semantics, which was not verified; [Verified] either way the vanilla body does not execute on that pass.
